## 11. Open rules questions and recommended rulings

Every place this spec decided something the source left open is listed here; each row is a config value or a named test in the engine. Rows marked decide change gameplay materially and are still open: [[R1]], [[R4]], [[R5]], [[R14]], [[R26]] and [[R39]] ([[R2]] was decided by patch v0.2.0, [[R389]]). The spec reviews of 2026-09-17 added [[R58]] to [[R90]]: [[R58]]–[[R75]] in the first round, [[R76]]–[[R81]] in the second, [[R82]] and the refinements to [[R33]], [[R43]], [[R61]], [[R64]], [[R73]], [[R76]], [[R77]], [[R79]], [[R80]] and [[R81]] in the third and fourth, and [[R83]]–[[R90]] in the milestone reviews of the same day, along with edits to [[§2.5]], [[§3]], [[§4]], [[§4.4]], [[§4.5]], [[§5.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§8]], [[§9.5]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.2]], [[§10.6]], [[§10.7]] and [[§10.8]]: the designer chose [[R59]], [[R60]], [[R61]] and the Transmogulate pool in [[R35]], and the rest follow the source text or Hearthstone.

**[[R91]] to [[R170]] were added on 2026-09-18**, and this paragraph did not name them until it was corrected — which is why the provenance is now stated as a rule rather than a list: every row from [[R91]] on was added by the milestone work or by a review of it, in the same change as the code it describes and the test that pins it (`fn r<n>_…` in Rust, `it("R<n> …")` in TypeScript), and `cargo jackioh spec check` fails if either is missing. The sections they edit, beyond those above, are [[§4.3]] ([[R93]]'s First Strike, stated attacker-only against [[§6.1]] until Part A r5 caught it), [[§9.1]], [[§9.3]], [[§9.4]], [[§9.5]], [[§10.3]], [[§10.4]], [[§10.5]], [[§10.9]] ([[R102]], [[R113]], [[R116]], [[R126]] and [[R153]] all needed `resume`, `delayed` and `setStat`, which its `Script` listing omitted) and [[§10.8]] ([[R168]], [[R169]]). None of these is a designer choice: they are rulings the code had to make to run, written down after the fact where the spec was silent, and where one contradicted working code — [[R93]], [[R119]] — the code was right and the text was the defect.

**[[R171]] on are the polish pass of 2026-09-22** (`docs/polish/`), under the same rule. Each task was given a block of numbers so the branches could be built at once, and a block need not be used up, so the table has gaps: [[R171]]–[[R179]] and, past the blocks, [[R209]]–[[R226]] and [[R240]]–[[R243]] are the edge-case hunt (`docs/polish/4-edge-cases.md`), whose rounds filled its own block first. The sections it edits are [[§4.1]] ([[R171]]), [[§4.4]] ([[R240]]), [[§8]] #64's Engine cell ([[R213]]), [[§10.1]], [[§10.5]] and [[§10.7]].

**[[R244]] on are features added after the polish pass**, under the same rule. [[R244]] and [[R245]] are The Coin ([[§2.1]], [[§2.3]], [[§5]], [[§7]], [[§9.9]]), which the user asked for on 2026-09-24.

**[[R247]] is the live-cards change of 2026-09-24**, from the block [[R247]]–R249: what a card face shows in play against what the collection prints ([[§10.10]]). It edits [[§8]] #82's Engine cell ([[R247]]) and [[§10.10]].

**[[R250]] to [[R264]] are the decks-and-modes change of 2026-09-24**, which the user asked for: named decks and trios in place of the one three-deck loadout, deck codes and autosave ([[§2.6]], [[§9.1]], [[§9.4]]), and three queue modes with the Best-of-3 series ([[§9.5]], [[§9.8]], [[§9.9]]). Where the brief left a choice open — what a drawn game in a series counts for, how a series is rated, what happens when nobody picks — the row says so and gives the reason.

**[[R265]] on are the concurrent mulligan and the draw offer of 2026-09-24**, from the block [[R265]]–R274, which the user asked for: both players mulligan at the same time ([[R265]]–[[R268]]), a draw offer's lifetime ([[R269]]), and the server's own nonces, which the review of the mulligan clock found a client could pre-empt ([[R270]]). They edit [[§2.1]], [[§2.5]], [[§9.5]], [[§9.9]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.2]], [[§10.6]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R275]] on are the Radiant pass of 2026-09-24**, from the block [[R275]]–R289, which the user asked for the same day: every Radiant face held to one power standard and the cards below it raised (`docs/radiant-audit.md`), the Radiant face printed in full with what differs from the base marked, the Jlockeed tag, the reference map a card's text links through, and the numbers a formula comes to now. It edits [[§5]], [[§5.2]], [[§6.1]], [[§7]], [[§8]] (27 rows' Radiant or Stats cells, #13 and #14's tags, and the Engine cells of #33, #38, #50, #67, #80 and #96), [[§10.8]], [[§10.9]], [[§10.10]], [[R67]] and [[R74]].

**[[R653]] is patch v0.2.3 (issue #113, 2026-10-03)**, which the game's designer asked for: eighteen Field
Spells that could come to life gain Animated with a unit face ([[§8]]'s rows say which), and C+ #33
Ivory Tower's text becomes "The first Unit you stack onto this is fused into it". [[R653]] settles what the
issue left to Hearthstone's semantics: when the stacked Unit is fused in, what is fused when an answer
changed it, and how long "the first" lasts. It takes the next number after [[R652]] ([[R651]] and [[R652]] having
been taken by patch v0.2.2's Joro and Blade Storm rulings while this branch was in flight), and the
patch edits [[§3.2]], [[§5.1]], [[§6.1]], [[§7]], [[§8]] (C+ #5, C+ #24, C+ #33, T-AI-6 and the eighteen cards) and [[R5]],
[[R13]], [[R78]], [[R383]], [[R408]], [[R418]], [[R446]], [[R447]] and [[R588]]. Undone by patch v0.2.5 (issue #218), except Ivory Tower's fusion, which stays; the patch also adds [[R657]].

**[[R654]] is the public card and player statistics page of the v0.2.X patch (issue #131, 2026-10-03)**:

under which AI development games pad the card win-rate figures, and at and above which public statistics
strictly use live games only and AI games are ignored; the source labeling and provisional progress banner;
the minimum per-row sample threshold (`CARD_STATS_MIN_SAMPLE`, 20 games) below which win rates show
"not enough games" rather than a percentage; the public player summaries with player privacy opt-out;
and the server cache TTLs (`CARD_STATS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, 300 s; `PLAYER_STATS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`, 60 s).
It takes the next number after [[R653]]. It edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1438]] to [[R1440]] and [[R1444]] are Meditative batch 27, the gacha pulls (issue #571, 2026-10-09)**, the three cards the designer added after the brief, M #100 Greaser, M #101 Gachaholic and M #102 Catboy Maid SSR+, and the rule the designer added with them: what counts as a Luck-based card, the non-token cards that print Lucky or flip a coin, read off the definition (MD-G1, [[R1444]]); Lucky given to a card, a granted keyword put on in the hand, added to its printed Lucky and read by every pool card's roll (MD-G2, [[R1438]]); and a coin flip that takes Lucky, heads its better side unless the card says otherwise, one draw with no Lucky so that no shipped game changes (MD-G4, [[R1440]]). [[R1439]] is MD-G3, Catboy Maid SSR+'s two Lucky rolls, whose note came with its script in the release (issue #553), once ME-JADE (issue #525) had landed. M10 had reserved its blocks before this part existed, so it takes the next free numbers, past those other branches in flight hold (`docs/issues-and-patches.md`, Ruling numbers); the Luck-based reading, R1437 and then R1442 on its branch, became [[R1444]] when main gave those numbers to the result write and the play telemetry. They edit [[§5]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§8.1]], [[§8.6]], [[§8.8]], [[R32]], [[R130]], [[R381]], [[R902]] and [[R1420]].

**[[R1432]] to [[R1436]] are player usernames (issue #579, 2026-10-09)**, shown everywhere a player is named: a username's form, a base name of letters, digits and marks in one script with a numeric tag when it is taken, read in NFKC form ([[R1432]]); the light profanity filter, the only content check ([[R1433]]); uniqueness by NFKC case fold, the lowest free tag, and the `Player#n` default every account starts with ([[R1434]]); changing the name once a day through the server's preview, and the prompt a newly active account answers or skips ([[R1435]]); and the profile id that keys a player everywhere beneath the username shown, which replaces the old tag derived from it ([[R1436]]). They take the next numbers after [[R1431]], above the block held for #496. They edit [[§9.1]], [[§9.4]], [[§9.11]], [[§9.12]], [[R612]] and [[R654]].

**[[R1437]] is a result the store fails to take (issue #621, 2026-10-10)**: the match actor tries the write again, and a write that never lands leaves the match `live`, both players still in it, for the next socket's rebuild to record. It takes the next number after [[R1436]]. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1441]] is the match socket's heartbeat (issue #622, 2026-10-10)**: the server pings an attached match socket and drops one that has sent nothing for three intervals, which starts its seat's grace; its outgoing queue is bounded and a socket that overflows it is closed; and a page that wakes probes a quiet socket and replaces it if it does not answer, with jitter on the reconnect backoff. It takes R1441, the first number free on `main` and on the open branches: R1437 is issue #621's, and issue #571's branch holds R1438 to R1440. It edits [[§9.5]] and [[§9.8]].

**[[R1442]] is the play telemetry (issue #637, part 1 of #636, 2026-10-10)**: each live match's think times, emotes and end-of-game signals, kept by match and seat in three server-only tables, written after the result and never at its expense, backfilled from the action logs still held, purged a year after the match and deleted with an account, and fitted into per-bucket think-time parameters that leave the AI's moves out. It takes R1442, the next number after [[R1441]]. It edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1443]] is signing in with a username (issue #645, 2026-10-10)**: the sign-in form takes an email or a username, and a username goes to the server, which finds the one profile holding it and asks the auth provider for the session with that profile's address, answering the session and never the address; every refusal is one sentence, and tries are limited per address and per username. It takes R1443, the first number free on `main` and on the open branches: issue #571's and issue #637's branches hold R1442. It edits [[§9.1]], [[§9.4]] and [[R1436]].

**[[R1445]] is a granted ability's quotation (the Meditative integration's house-style pass, 2026-10-11)**: [[R366]]'s labeled abilities that start their line are the card's own, and an ability a card grants another, Meditative #58's and #59's granted Deaths ([[R1107]]), is quoted inside the sentence that grants it, in double quotes, as #38 and C+ #14 quote theirs. It takes the next number after [[R1442]], R1439 being held for MD-G3. It edits nothing in the sections: [[§8]]'s cells are shorthand, and the catalog prints the quotation.

**[[R1446]] is the perfect cards' pool (issue #577, patch v0.3.6, 2026-10-11)**: #97 Zephrys and C+ #27 Zephrys Zealotism draw on every non-token card of every set that ships but their own, the designer's decision in the issue against the source notes' set limits, so a set joins both the day it ships. It takes the next number after [[R1445]]. It edits [[§5]], [[§8]], [[§10.7]], [[R29]], [[R380]], [[R387]] and [[R416]].

**[[R1447]] is two hidden-information leaks the daily super run's fuzz found (#659, 2026-10-11)**: a preview value's `ids` named the controller's own face-down traps to the other seat, and a Heroic Power on a face-down card (a Trap fused with #98) was listed in its hero's powers, naming the hidden card, though a face-down card has no ability to use. A preview's ids now keep only the cards the viewer reads, its count unchanged, and a face-down card's power is listed for neither seat. It takes R1447, the next number after [[R1446]]. It edits [[§10.8]], [[R43]] and [[R372]].

**[[R1448]] is the voice lines' update (issue #103, 2026-10-11)**: every Unit, tokens included, gets an attack line of four words at most, spoken where [[R655]] already plays the attack hook; three lines that repeated another card's are rewritten; the voice budget rises from [[R501]]'s 6 MiB to 12 MiB for the Meditative set and the attack lines; and the `voice` workflow renders the set on GitHub's macOS and Windows runners, so nobody needs either machine. It takes R1448, the next number after [[R1447]]. It edits [[§10.11]] and [[R501]].
**[[R1449]] is a hero portrait's two presses (the user's request, 2026-10-11)**: a click on a portrait, and Enter or Space on it, hangs the hero's menu off the portrait again, the emote menu or the opponent's mute item as before [[R1330]], and a hold, a touch's or a mouse's, a right-click, or the context-menu key or Shift+F10, opens the hero's inspect view with the same menu in it; targeting still wins. It takes R1449: [[R1446]] went to #674 while this was open, R1447 went to #685, and R1448 is held by the voice pull request. It edits [[§10.10]], [[R643]], [[R1330]] and [[R1331]].

**[[R1450]] is the reaper's sweep of open rows (issue #666, 2026-10-11)**: a room or a reserved match id still `open` five minutes past its `ceiling_at` (a room's expiry, a reservation's claim) is deleted, which frees the room code and both players, so a failed start whose cleanup failed too no longer locks two players out for good. It takes R1450, the first number free on `main` and on the open branches: issue #638's branch holds R1447 to R1449. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1451]], [[R1452]], [[R1453]] and [[R1454]] are the ladder bots (issue #638, part 2 of #636, 2026-10-11)**: while the server's `LADDER_BOTS` is `on`, a ranked Best of 1 or All Random ticket that has waited past its fallback time with no person inside its window is seated against a bot the server plays itself. They are the bot's seat, which plays the practice AI's search at a strength of its own on this spec's resources, at a person's pace and with a persona of its own, and which nothing a client reads tells from a person's ([[R1452]]); the empty-queue fallback that seats it ([[R1453]]); the pool, sized to the people who played in the last week, with its retirement, the bots' names, schedules and account ages, and their decks ([[R1454]]); and the statistics of a game against a bot, whose human side counts like any live game's and whose bot side is read only when a query asks for it ([[R1451]]). They took the next numbers after [[R1446]] while this branch was open, and moved up as `main` took [[R1447]] (issue #659), [[R1448]] (issue #103), [[R1449]] (the hero portrait) and [[R1450]] (issue #666): the statistics take R1451, the seat R1452, the fallback R1453 and the pool R1454, the first numbers free on `main` and on the open branches. They edit [[§9.5]], [[§9.9]], [[§9.11]], [[§9.12]], [[R376]], [[R610]], [[R611]], [[R645]], [[R654]], [[R744]], [[R1344]] and [[R1442]].

**[[R639000]] closes the ladder bots' tells (issue #639, 2026-10-12)**: a ladder bot's seat now concedes at the start of its turn when far enough behind, answers a draw offer at a person's speed and accepts only while behind, lets an occasional turn run out, waits longer on a busy board, a winning turn and its mulligan, shows its aim a beat before a targeted move, emotes by persona weights and delays of its own, and after a ranked game stays a while and may offer or meet a rematch; it still never offers a draw, and [[R188]] holds for `decide` and the practice AI. Every rate and window is a named row that `jackioh-server timing-fit` refits from [[R1442]]'s telemetry of people's play, a hand-set prior until then, and every roll draws on a stream of its own outside the state, so a match with a bot still replays from `(seed, log)`. It takes R639000, the first number of issue #639's block ([[R690000]]). It edits [[§9.5]], [[§9.9]], [[§9.11]], [[§9.12]], [[R188]], [[R645]], [[R1442]], [[R1452]] and [[R1453]].

**[[R640000]], [[R640001]] and [[R640002]] put the bots on the ladder and the queue's readout on counted numbers (issue #640, part 4 of #636, 2026-10-12)**: a ladder bot now holds a season row its ranked games move as a person's, shown on the leaderboard and the match screen as a player's, while Jlorious and the percentiles read people only, so a bot that would qualify stays Mythic Grape ([[R640000]]); the rating window stops at `MAX_FAIR_GAP` instead of going uncapped after 60 s, so past it the empty-queue fallback seats a bot rather than a mismatched person, and the terms say that ranked may pair a player with an AI opponent ([[R640001]]); and the lobby's count of open tickets gives way to each mode's expected wait and the last hour's games, every number one the server counted ([[R640002]]). They take R640000 to R640002, the first numbers of issue #640's block ([[R690000]]). They edit [[§9.5]], [[§9.12]], [[R257]], [[R505]], [[R605]], [[R608]], [[R610]], [[R611]] and [[R1452]].

**[[R641000]] seats the ladder bots in Conquest (issue #641, part 5 of #636, 2026-10-12)**: a Conquest ticket past its fallback time is paired with a ladder bot that holds a trio the pool dealt it, three decks a player could queue sharing no card, and the two play a ranked series. The claim and the series land in one transaction, and the series holds the bot between games, so no other claim seats it until the series ends; the bot makes each sealed pick through the same transition as a person's, 4 to 20 s after the pick phase opens, by the matchup's rule (its fastest deck on the play, its slowest on the draw) three times in four and uniformly otherwise; the series is rated once when it ends, its bot side as the bot it is; and a series game draws the bot no rematch plan. The issue's ghost results between two bots are not built: they wait on a person's decision once issue #640's changes are live. It takes R641000, the first number of issue #641's block ([[R690000]]). It edits [[§9.5]], [[§9.12]], [[R639000]], [[R640001]], [[R1452]], [[R1453]] and [[R1454]].

**[[R690000]] is the ruling numbers' new scheme (issue #690, 2026-10-12)**: every branch used to take the next free number, so two branches open at once took the same one and the second to merge renumbered its note, its tests and its citations. Now each issue owns a block of a thousand numbers, R(N×1000) to R(N×1000+999), so branches on different issues never meet, and every number before it stands. It takes R690000, the first number of issue #690's block. It edits `spec/README.md` (Note format, Adding a ruling) and CLAUDE.md rule 3.

**[[R724000]] and [[R724001]] are the Patch notes page by release line and the player note (issue #724, 2026-10-12)**: the page printed each patch's record, its title, source and notes, which name issues, rulings and commits, so players read the history's bookkeeping. Now every patch carries a player note apart from its record, a short title and one fact per line under New, Balance, Changes, Card text and Fixes, which a fragment holds by hand, `patches check` holds to words players can read and `patches ship` carries; the 28 shipped patches' notes were written by hand from their snapshot diffs ([[R724001]]). The page groups the patches by release line, one bubble per set (Core, Classic & Classic+, Meditative), newest first and only the newest open, each with a summary and the technical changes of its major version, its lettered patches beside the others ([[R724000]]). They take R724000 and R724001, the first numbers of issue #724's block ([[R690000]]). They edit [[§10.10]], [[R388]], [[R507]] and [[R646]].

**[[R732000]] and [[R732001]] are the AI with the Meditative set and the training lanes after it (issue #732, 2026-10-12)**, which the owner asked for in place of #616: the gate of [[R1390]] is lifted on a sweep of record over the Meditative set's 102 cards, the shadow ban holds Meditative cards only and at most half of them, never again a card of an earlier set ([[R732000]]), and once that AI had beaten random in at least 90 of 100 the training lanes stopped playing random, measuring a candidate against its parent alone at 65 of 100 for `improve` and 60 for `unban` ([[R732001]]). They take the first numbers of issue #732's block. They edit [[R1390]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R747000]] gives a ladder bot's strength a skill and measures its Elo (issue #747, part of #636, 2026-10-13)**: one AI ships, and a weaker bot is that AI degraded by a number that knows nothing of its internals: a skill from 0 to 1, the share of a seat's decisions that follow the AI, the rest a random legal action rolled on the seat's own stream, so 1 plays the AI exactly as before with no extra draw and 0 is the random policy. The match harness, the arena (`--a-skill`, `--b-skill`) and the ladder-bot seat take it, the five strengths carry 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75 and 1 beside the budgets, temperatures and missed-lethal chances they had, and the skill-to-Elo curve is measured with arena games against the reference and against random, kept as a table the server reads (`SKILL_ELO_TABLE`, `expected_elo_for_skill`) with the protocol that measures it again when the AI or a set changes. A strength's seed rating is the curve's reading at its skill, so the ladder's seed ratings now run from 570 to the 1200 the strongest bot had; live Glicko ratings stay the calibration. It takes R747000, the first number of issue #747's block ([[R690000]]). It edits [[§9.12]], [[R1452]] and [[R1454]].

**[[R757000]] and [[R757001]] are the age screen and the terms a player agrees to (issue #757, 2026-10-13)**, from a pre-launch checklist the owner shared: sign-up asked no age and showed "By creating an account you agree…" as a hint, which recorded nothing. Now sign-up asks the month and year of birth with nothing chosen, refuses an answer under `MIN_ACCOUNT_AGE_YEARS` neutrally and remembers it on the device, and the server counts the age again, deletes an account under the minimum and keeps only that the check passed ([[R757000]]); an unticked box agrees to the Terms and the Privacy Policy, and each agreement is kept per account and version, accounts made before it holding a baseline and asked once ([[R757001]]). Redemption refuses an account that owes either. The minimum is the owner's to choose (13 is built; 16 if EU players are expected). They take the first numbers of issue #757's block. They edit [[§9.4]].

**[[R776000]] is a clock's action the store fails to write, and an actor its log has moved past (issue #776, 2026-10-13)**: a timeout, a disconnect forfeit or the ceiling's draw whose row the store refused ran once and was lost, so the match stayed stuck until the reaper's ceiling. Now its clock runs out again a second later until the row lands. The log answers a repeated nonce at its own seq with that row, in the in-memory store as in Postgres, and a clash at the actor's next seq makes the actor let go as [[R1437]]'s does. It takes R776000, the first number of issue #776's block. It edits [[§9.3]] and [[§9.5]].

**[[R750000]] is a ladder bot's decks leaning on what wins (issue #750, part of #636, 2026-10-13)**: people's Best-of-1 lists with at least 20 live games go first, ranked by win rate and taken in the order the bot's strength share says (the best first for the strongest bot, the worst first for the weakest), ahead of the lists still shuffled from the bot's seed, and a bot's random decks weigh each card by its compiled in-deck win rate, as far as the bot's strength share says (none for the weakest bot, the full `AI_WIN_RATE.max_boost` for the strongest). Every player's deal and every deal the AI makes for itself passes no strength and deals the deck it always did. It takes R750000, the first number of issue #750's block. It edits [[R1454]] and [[R641000]]'s trio deal.

**[[R513000]] is what a shared GIF holds (issue #513, part 5 of #430, 2026-10-13)**: the replay player on its dev-only route can export the shown turn, or a range marked on the scrubber, as a GIF. Its frames are drawn from the clip's steps' views alone ([[R768]]): a face-down card is its back, each hand a count, the seats their portraits ([[R641]]) labeled "You" and "Opponent", and no account name anywhere. The plan is a pure function of the clip and its options on a fixed frame clock, so the same clip and options give the same plan and the same bytes; a GIF over the byte cap is drawn smaller, then slower, then refused. It takes R513000, the first number of issue #513's block. It edits [[§9.3]].

**[[R748000]] is the ladder bots' strengths, calibrated (issue #748, part of #636, 2026-10-13)**: the skill curve ([[R747000]]) seeds each strength from the AI at the arena's budget, which leaves the strengths' own budgets, temperatures and missed-lethal chances unmeasured. Now the strongest is the reference: `bot-calibrate` plays every pair 30 or more games as the bots play (skill included) and fits each strength's offset from it, `bot-calibrate-live` measures the reference against people's ratings, the printed seed is the anchor plus the offset, and after an AI promotion the anchor moves by the promotions' `vsParent` Elo and the games are played again. The first calibration agrees with the curve in order and spread and puts its two lowest rows 66 and 82 Elo higher; the seeds stay at the curve, and moving its rows is a person's decision. A player rated above every bot is still never given one ([[R1453]]), and live R610 ratings go on correcting the pool. It takes R748000, the first number of issue #748's block. It edits [[§9.12]] and [[R1454]].

**[[R783000]] to [[R783013]] are five new keywords, built ahead of any card (issue #783, part 1 of #781, 2026-10-13)**: the designer's Ranged, Flying, Cloaked, Crater and Flashback, with the designer's answers in #781, work in the engine, the AI and the client, proven on engine fixture cards, while no catalog card prints them, no pool draws them and every game the catalog can play replays as before; part 2 prints them. Ranged takes no strike back ([[R783000]]), and Cleave adds nothing to that ([[R783001]]); Flying is attacked only by Flying or Ranged ([[R783002]]) and lands in Defense Position ([[R783003]]); Cloaked sets a card that is not a Trap as one ([[R783004]]) at its own price ([[R783005]]), revealed only by its Activate: Reveal ([[R783006]]), an aura may lend it to the cards in a hand ([[R783007]]), a set card leaving unrevealed is its printed card ([[R783008]]), and the AI's worlds sample it ([[R783009]]); Crater Locks a Unit's zone as it arrives ([[R783010]]) and a Trap's as it is revealed ([[R783011]]); Flashback plays a card from its graveyard once ([[R783012]]) and is then gone for good ([[R783013]]). Where the designer's text and answers left a mechanic open the notes follow Hearthstone and the least frustrating reading; [[R783001]] and [[R783005]] answer the issue's two open questions, for the designer to confirm. They take R783000 to R783013 from issue #783's block. They edit [[§4.2]], [[§4.3]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.2]], [[§6.3]], [[§10.2]] and [[§10.4]].

**[[R781000]] to [[R781005]] are the keyword update's switch-on (issue #781, part 2 of 2 after #783, patch v0.3.7, 2026-10-13)**: Magnetic is renamed Equip, its old name kept as a read-only alias for the shipped snapshots and the logs recorded before ([[R781000]]); Immutable keeps its text half and gains a targeting half, so while in play Spells, Field Spells and Field Traps can't target it, while Units, Traps that are not Field Traps, attacks, random picks and "all" effects still reach it ([[R781001]]), and a Field Trap whose trigger names it fires and passes it by ([[R781002]]); a Vanilla returns a card to its printed stats ([[R781003]]); Keymaster Keenus draws random combat keywords ([[R781004]]) from the pool, now named and eighteen strong ([[R781005]]). The same patch prints the new keywords: Knowledge Breaker and its Prime lend your Units Cloaked ([[R1040]] to [[R1043]]), Paranoia lends your Spells Cloaked and its Radiant Echo is read as a Spell reveals ([[R1044]], [[R1045]]), ME-ALTPLAY's face-down permission going with them; Cloaked Toe Cracker prints Cloaked ([[R783005]]) and Doom Shroom Crater ([[R783011]]). The designer answered the questions in four rounds on the issue.

**[[R817000]] is a random deck's cap on Quickdraw cards (issue #817, 2026-10-14)**, which the owner asked for as less Quickdraw bloat: the deck builder dealt as many Quickdraw cards as its draw gave, and a deck it themed on Quickdraw, a tag of ten cards and so a theme, at least six. Now every deck it deals holds at most two (`AI_DECK.maxQuickdraw`), the cards an `include` forces in counted and always dealt, and Quickdraw is never a theme, so some seeds roll another theme and the tutorial tier's frozen run ([[R290]]) was frozen again on a new series. It takes R817000, the first number of issue #817's block. It edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R822000]] retires the Mythic themes and the shared Legendary entrance themes (issue #822, 2026-10-14)**: a card's own intro was followed by a theme taking over the music, and the two clashed. Now no card plays a theme: the intro plays over the result, low-health and station tracks, and the station switches stay. The 17 theme tracks are deleted, their music kept in the Mythics' intros, which were hand-tuned from them. Presentation only. It takes R822000, the first number of issue #822's block, and edits [[§10.11]], [[R631]], [[R1350]], [[R1351]] and [[R1352]].

**[[R821000]] is a face-down card's prompt option naming the zone it lies in (issue #821, 2026-10-14)**: "Place N Plague Counters" ([[R689]], [[R471]]) opened a list of every permanent on the field, over the board it is answered on. Now it is answered by a click on the highlighted card or zone, and a face-down card of the other player, which has no id in the view, is clicked by the zone its back lies in, so a prompt option that offers a card its chooser may not read ([[R177]]) carries that public position as well as its id. Nothing hidden leaves the engine and the answer is the one the list gave. It takes R821000, the first number of issue #821's block.

**[[R824000]] is a number only the Radiant face prints (issue #824, 2026-10-14)**: thirty-one such numbers on thirty cards were still tuned on both faces; each is now tuned on the Radiant face only, as [[R749]] did for two and [[R1431]] for every number only the base face prints, and `catalog check` refuses one left unmarked. It edits [[§6.3]]'s Nerf row, [[§8.6]]'s Tunes paragraph, C+ #32.3's row in [[§8.7]], [[R386]], [[R482]], [[R652]] and [[R749]].

**[[R831000]] to [[R831016]] are the Meditative patch notes' first round (issue #831, patch v0.3.10, 2026-10-15)**: the designer's balance, text and behavior changes for some fifty cards, most of them Meditative. Where the notes leave a decision open the change follows Hearthstone and a ruling says so: Silly Silas rotates into Locked zones ([[R831000]]); Palantir casts the (1) Spell it steals, every choice random and aimed ([[R831001]]); the night market sells each lot on its Radiant face too ([[R831002]]); KY's Papaya Discovers each cell among 3 and announces its curve in a public `curveDrawn` event ([[R831003]]); KY's Trial Discovers a set, then a number in it ([[R831004]]); Prestige makes the next card played this turn Radiant ([[R831005]]); Windfast's substitute is a random Unit from hand ([[R831006]]); Tesla zaps only the Unit that reveals it ([[R831007]]); Showdown asks for an empty lane, all four zones empty ([[R831008]]); The Power to Thrive's card is chosen as it resolves ([[R831009]]); Tommy Tempo loses the mana left once its price is paid ([[R831010]]); Twice Forward is Animated and fuses a copy, never the card ([[R831011]]); Ever Growing Tree offers 2 Field Spells ([[R831012]]); Jilliax is Equip only at its (3) price ([[R831013]]); Eugenics exiles every deck card costing (2) or less per [[R65]] ([[R831014]]); one Corpse Plantation Activate arms one graveyard play ([[R831015]]); and Plague Crawler may place on itself ([[R831016]]). It edits [[§8.3]], [[§8.6]], [[§8.7]] and [[§8.8]]'s rows of those cards, [[§5.1]]'s tag note, [[§6.1]]'s Equip row, [[R14]], [[R54]], [[R88]], [[R247]], [[R422]], [[R425]], [[R514]], [[R589]], [[R688]], [[R945]], [[R1000]], [[R1086]], [[R1103]], [[R1104]], [[R1202]], [[R1421]], [[R1444]] and [[R781002]]. CN Flea Market's ten new shop cards are left to the designer.

**[[R833000]] is what the reaper does with a match whose log already ends (issue #833, 2026-10-14)**: a match whose result write never landed ([[R1437]]) and that no socket came back to sat `live` until the ceiling, and the reaper then drew it, overwriting the real outcome with no rating move, last board, final hash or game record. Now the reaper folds the log of each match past its ceiling once and, when it ends in a result, records that result as the actor would have; any other match is drawn as [[R112]] says. It takes R833000, the first number of issue #833's block, and edits [[§9.5]], [[R112]] and [[R1437]].

**[[R849000]] marks a summoning-sick unit and says why a unit cannot attack (issue #849, 2026-10-14)**: a summoning-sick unit looked like any other, and clicking it did nothing. `viewFor` now gives every unit `sick: true` while it is summoning sick with neither Rush nor Charge, and the viewer's own unit that may act `attackReason`, the refusal [[§4.2]] step 1 gives it. Polish task 6 had dropped Hearthstone's zzz because `canAct` cannot say "asleep". Presentation only. It takes R849000, the first number of issue #849's block, and edits [[§10.8]].

**[[R850000]] and [[R850001]] are the improve lane's measuring protocol (issue #850, 2026-10-14)**: in 36 sessions the lane never came near 65 of 100 against its parent, its kept changes were picked on the very seeds that measured them, and every game had its own deal, so the deal's luck was never canceled. Now its gate plays 200 hold-out deals, each twice with the seats swapped, and promotes on 220 of the 400 games, about two deviations above an equal successor and less with the luck pairing cancels ([[R850000]]); its screens play a fixed dev set the same way, at least 400 games against a control, keep a change only when the paired edge's lower bound is above zero, and never play the gate's deals, which only the committed AI they are named for reads ([[R850001]]). The unban lane's gate stays as it was. The owner asked for the lower bar in the issue. They take the first numbers of issue #850's block and edit [[R732001]].

**[[R767000]] is a replay folding under its game's catalog version (issue #767, part 6 of #430, 2026-10-14)**: a replay of a game played before a card patch was refused as `earlier_patch` after it. The build now carries every older shipped version's cards, rebuilt from the patch history's snapshots, and folds such a game under them; the final-hash check still refuses one the build cannot reproduce. It edits [[§9.3]] and [[R768]].

**[[R768000]] is where a replay opens (issue #768, part 7 of #430, 2026-10-14)**: replays reach players. The practice result dialog and an online match's result panel offer "Watch the replay" and "Share the last turn", and `/replays` lists the recent games. It edits [[§9.5]], [[§9.6]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R915000]] to [[R915010]] are custom cards (issue #915, v0.5.0, 2026-10-15)**: a player makes cards of their own, a frame and a script in the card kit, and plays them in their own deck against the AI. The new section [[§12]] states them: the id space and the definition ([[R915000]]), the frame's limits ([[R915001]]), the kit and the host the engine is lent for it ([[R915002]]), questions mid-script answered by running the hook again ([[R915003]]), a script that fails ([[R915004]]), practice only ([[R915005]]), replays ([[R915006]]), the AI ([[R915007]]), the account that keeps them ([[R915008]]), the Luau module that runs them ([[R915009]]) and the creator they are made in ([[R915010]]).

**[[R923000]] to [[R923003]] are practice's sandbox (issue #923, 2026-10-14)**: a practice game started with the Sandbox box accepts one more action, `sandbox`, whose tools spawn any shipped card for either seat into any zone it may stand in, placed and never played ([[R923001]]), set a hero's health and Armor or a player's mana and draw ([[R923002]]), and destroy or remove a card ([[R923003]]). Only a game created as a sandbox accepts it, `legalActions` never lists it and a server match refuses it ([[R923000]]). It adds a paragraph to [[§9.9]].

**[[R924000]] lets "More cards from" pick the set (issue #924, 2026-10-14)**: [[R1372]]'s and [[R1373]]'s switch leaned a random deck on the newest set alone. It is now a picker of none, "newest" (the newest set that ships when the deck is dealt, as before) or any set that ships, on All Random's queue, rooms and rematches and on practice's Random deck; migration 0036 turns every stored switch into the pick it meant. It edits [[§9.5]], [[§9.9]], [[R1372]] and [[R1373]].

**[[R884000]] bounds an Activate N and University's Buffs (issue #884, 2026-10-15)**: each use of M #97.6 University's `study` Buffs it too, and a Buff can step both its Activate N and its `times`, so every use funded more uses and more Buffs per use until one turn never ended (batch 18's stalled game, `docs/v0.4.0/README.md` §5.8). An Activate N now counts at most `ACTIVATE_UNLIMITED_CAP` uses a turn, as ♾️ does, and University's `times` is never above 5, its Radiant value. It takes R884000, the first number of issue #884's block, and edits [[§6.2]] and [[§8.8]].

The table's rows are the ruling notes in `rulings/`, one per row (`rulings/R0195.md` is [[R195]]), each with its ruling, the cards and sections it affects, and the tests that prove it. [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) lists them all, one line each. The paragraphs below are appended by `cargo jackioh spec fold`, which `.github/workflows/spec-fold.yml` runs on `main` daily: a branch writes its own to `pending/<n>.md` and never this file's end ([[README]], Adding a ruling).

**[[R835000]] to [[R835002]] close the two gaps in [[§9.8]]'s abuse surface (issue #835, patch v0.3.X)**: refusals past a per-seat threshold inside a window raise one `match.rejections.unusual` alert per match per seat ([[R835000]]); queued-but-unrun `hello` frames coalesce per socket generation, so a hello flood costs at most one outstanding view push and a reattach's hello always runs ([[R835001]]); and malformed frames past a per-seat budget close that seat's socket with 1008, starting its disconnect grace ([[R835002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #835's block ([[R690000]]). They edit [[§9.5]] and [[§9.8]].

**[[R897000]] is the queue ticket a closed client abandoned (issue #897, patch v0.3.X)**: every signed-in request refreshes its caller's presence, and each sweep first cancels every open ticket unheard from for longer than `QUEUE_TICKET_STALE_MS` (12 s), so an absent player seats no person and no ladder bot, is never charged the disconnect grace, and takes no phantom ranked loss; a restarted server judges nothing until it has listened one window. `GET /api/auth/me` answers `queued` while the caller holds an open ticket, and a client that reads it gone twice in a row stops waiting with a rejoin message. It takes R897000, the first number of issue #897's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R890000]] is a match seat claimed atomically (issue #890, patch v0.3.X)**: the seat write was check-then-act across transactions with no guard, so a room join racing a queue claim seated one player in two matches, and ending the first orphaned the second into an unseen ranked loss. Now the seat is compare-and-set — the first writer wins — and each starter maps the loss onto its failed-start cleanup: the queue drops its seats and records the failed start, a room join answers 409 and frees the code, a rematch or series game stops and voids the started match ([[R679]]) while the taken seat keeps its game. A host holds at most three open rooms. It takes R890000, the first number of issue #890's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R910000]] is which words a card's text prints in bold (issue #910, part 3 of #814, patch v0.3.X)**: a keyword bolded at the start of a sentence stayed plain in the middle of one ("Exile" bolded, "exile it" did not; "steal it" stayed plain by [[R512]]), and Lizard's Breath bolded its Exile pile beside a plain Deck and Graveyard. Now every [[§6.2]] trigger and [[§6.3]] verb bolds in every case and inflection the cards print, the exile pile and Corpse Plantation's "counter" stay plain, [[§6.1]] keywords bold as the keywords a card has, and the [[§6.3]] rows the glossary keeps rules-only (Summon, Draw, Discard, Destroy and the like) stay plain, as Hearthstone prints them. The web's tests fail on any card that breaks it. It edits [[§6]].

**[[R962000]] is a sandbox game's own custom cards (issue #962, patch v0.3.X)**: the sandbox spawned only cards of a set that ships ([[R923001]]), and a custom card's set never ships, so its author could not place it on a board built by hand. Now a sandbox game whose setup carries custom cards spawns those cards, and only those: a custom id the game was not given is refused like an unshipped card. It takes R962000, the first number of issue #962's block. It edits [[§9.9]] and [[§12.6]].

**[[R964000]] is a Duos seat's life as the AI counts it (issue #964, patch v0.3.X)**: the AI read only a seat's own HP, so a 25-damage enemy board read as lethal against 20 own HP and a 20 pool, and an untouched Duos opponent read as 10 damage down. Every life the AI reads is now own HP and the team's pool together, as [[§4.4]] step 4a's lethal judge counts them, and its hero value and closing term are anchored at the seat's starting life; a 1v1 game reads as before. It takes R964000, the first number of issue #964's block. It edits [[§13.7]].

**[[R976000]] deals an arena's and a promotion's two seats by one rule (issue #976, 2026-10-15)**: the deck builder weighted a seat by the unban lane's dealt-quality table whenever its ban list was this build's own, so the unban lane's candidate was always shaped and its parent never, and its 11 promotions passed at 68 to 84 of 100 while the improve lane, whose seats were dealt alike, never promoted. Shaping is now an explicit option, off unless a caller asks, and no caller does; the shaped mulligan stays. The bot proposed it and the owner approved it in the issue. It takes R976000, the first number of issue #976's block. It edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R977000]] is how the shadow-ban sweep times a decision (issue #977, 2026-10-15)**: the sweep counted a decision over 2 s by the raw wall clock, so a busy machine could ban a card. A decision its clock finds slow is now timed again as the timing gate times one, against the yardstick, and is a `timeout` only if still over; the sweep's lines and report carry the clock's count beside it. It takes R977000, the first number of issue #977's block. It edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R978000]] is the public card statistics kept for their cache TTL (issue #978, patch v0.3.X)**: both card routes read every record of a patch on every request, and the drill-down did so for each patch the server ships, on the pool live matches write through. Now the server computes a patch's figures once for each source and pilot, keeps them for `CARD_STATS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (what the response already tells browsers it may keep), computes them once however many requests miss together, and keeps at most `CARD_STATS_KEPT_REPORTS` of them, the one kept longest ago dropped first; the drill-down reads the same kept reports. A patch the server does not ship is never kept, and for the same records every response is unchanged. It takes R978000, the first number of issue #978's block. It edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R874000]] is the most cards one fusion holds (issue #874, patch v0.3.Y)**: Meditative #95 fuses a hand into one card and two copies of it, so a hand of those three fused again holds three times as many, and nothing bounded it: the daily fuzz's seed 8286 grew one fusion to 288 cards and the next ran the process out of memory. A fusion is now made only while its ingredients hold at most 64 cards, a fused ingredient counting the cards it was fused from; one past that fizzles like a fusion of fewer than two. It takes R874000, the first number of issue #874's block. It edits [[§6.3]] and [[R1242]].

**[[R1025000]] takes Armor and Spell Damage out of the combat keyword pool (issue #1025, patch v0.3.Y)**: the keyword overhaul of issue #781 meant the pool of [[R21]] and [[R781005]] to hold keywords with no number, and left Armor 1 and Spell Damage +1 in it. Plastic Surgery, Zao Gao, the Conjure Rush Tokens, a Buff's keyword row and Keymaster Keenus's Aura no longer draw either; the other sixteen keep their order. R781005 stays as the v0.3.7 record, as [[R346]], [[R636]] and [[R49]] stay beside R21. It takes R1025000, the first number of issue #1025's block. It edits [[§6.1]] and [[R21]].

**[[R970000]], [[R970001]] and [[R970002]] close the gaps an audit of the Terms of Service flow found (issue #970, 2026-10-15)**: `/replays`, outside the gate for its practice half, let an account owing the terms list and share its online games, and now asks the consent screen before its online half ([[R970000]]); a second change to a page on the day it already changed kept `TERMS_VERSION`, which now takes a letter for it, and a web test holds each version to the pages' words so a change cannot keep it by mistake ([[R970001]]); and the presence heartbeat, which every signed-in session sends, records nothing while the account owes the terms, the policy naming it for every signed-in account ([[R970002]]). The same issue brought custom cards and every submission into the Terms and the Privacy Policy, chat's rules into the Terms, and a read of the chat reports for the owner (`moderate chat-reports`, `docs/compliance.md`). They take the first numbers of issue #970's block. They edit [[§9.4]].

**[[R979000]] says why a card in hand cannot be played (issue #979, patch v0.3.X)**: a card in hand that `legalActions` offers no play of did nothing when clicked, while a unit that cannot attack says why ([[R849000]]) and an Activate control that is off does too ([[R384]]). `viewFor` now gives the viewer's own hand card `playReason` while they may act and no play is listed: the first refusal in a fixed order — a play ban, no price it can pay, no open zone, no legal Tribute set, no legal choice for a required declaration — in the engine's words, read off the functions the listing and the reducer already call. Presentation only. It takes R979000, the first number of issue #979's block, and edits [[§10.8]].

**[[R1044000]] and [[R1044001]] condense the card statistics (issue #1044, 2026-10-16)**: every figure read every record of a patch, and the records grew without end once the gather lane played without a cap, about 4,000 games and 7 MB a day. Each record is now folded, as it is written, into totals of counts every figure reads, by the counting a record file is read with, so every report is byte for byte what the records gave ([[R1044000]]); and once folded, a record's detail is kept a week and its id for good ([[R1044001]]). They amend [[R376]] and [[R978000]], take the first numbers of issue #1044's block, and edit [[§9.11]].

**[[R1045000]] makes K-Pop Fanatic Allure the enemy permanent it chooses (issue #1045, patch v0.3.Y)**: #50's Cry was the delayed steal Meditative #39.5 Jade Beauty's Allure makes of each enemy Unit, so it prints Allure, and Allure reaches every permanent: the target carries Allure's pink mark, and one with no open zone in its row on the taker's side is destroyed rather than left with the opponent. Jade Beauty still Allures Units only. It takes R1045000, the first number of issue #1045's block. It edits [[§6.3]], [[§8.2]], [[R282]], [[R437]], [[R963]] and [[R767000]].

**[[R1047000]] to [[R1047002]] bound a co-op match as [[§9.5]] bounds a 1v1 one (issue #1047, patch v0.3.X)**: a co-op game had no wall-clock bound, since nothing sent the `ceilingReached` that [[§13.4]]'s table promises a `ceiling` draw for; a live match whose actor could not be folded back held both people for ever, since only its own actor wrote its end; and a host could hold any number of open co-op rooms, where a 1v1 host holds three. The actor now ends the match as the `ceiling` draw `MATCH_CEILING_MS` after the row was made, the deadline derived from the row ([[R1047000]]); the sweep ends a match it cannot fold back once that deadline has passed, with the same result, which frees both people ([[R1047001]]); and a host's fourth open co-op room is refused, counted apart from their 1v1 rooms ([[R1047002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1047's block. They edit [[§13.4]] and [[§13.9]].

**[[R1065000]] holds a co-op match's players out of 1v1 games (issue #1065, patch v0.3.X)**: a 1v1 match kept its players out of co-op, but a co-op match, which sets no in-match flag, did not keep its players out of 1v1: a player who left a co-op game for the menus could queue, be paired into a ranked game and lose it to the disconnect grace, or open the 1v1 game while the round clock ended their co-op turns and left their teammate alone. A live co-op match now counts as "in a match" at every door into a 1v1 game: the queue, a room's open and join (a challenge's included), the host check when someone joins, the pairing sweep and its ladder-bot fallback, and a rematch. The refusal is `409 already_in_match` with the match in `details.duosMatchId`, as co-op's own, and a co-op start cancels both people's open tickets as a series' start does. One server check serves every 1v1 and co-op door. It takes R1065000, the first number of issue #1065's block. It edits [[§9.5]] and [[§13.9]].

**[[R1075000]] lets a room's host cancel it and find it again (issue #1075, patch v0.3.X)**: a 1v1 room's code lived only in the host's page, so a reload lost it while the room stayed joinable until it expired: joiners met "the host of that room is playing another game right now", and the room held one of the host's three open rooms. The host may now cancel a room nobody has joined (`DELETE /api/rooms/:code`, which frees the code at once, never touches a joined room, and answers `cancelled: false` the second time), and the lobby lists the host's open rooms (`GET /api/rooms`) and shows the newest as a ticket again after a reload, with a Cancel room button. A room a friend's challenge opened is canceled with its challenge. It takes R1075000, the first number of issue #1075's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1074000]] is how a duel's events judge a card passed out of it (issue #1074, 2026-10-17)**: the Duos fuzz, running the 1v1 fuzz's invariant monitor over each duel for the first time, found that a pass (B20), which takes a hand card out of its duel without an event, left the card in no pile, so `view_for` judged it as a card that ceased to exist and named its draw, and every event of its time in the passer's hand, to the passer's opponent. In a duel, such a card, a token included, now reads to the player whose hand the events last show it in and to nobody else, as [[§13.5]] has it, and a token the events show in no hand reads openly as [[R11]] has it; a card that left the hand by a public move is judged where it went, as before, and a 1v1 game is untouched. It takes R1074000, the first number of issue #1074's block. It edits [[§13.5]].

**[[R1081000]] lets Flying units stand in the backrow (issue #1081, patch v0.3.Y)**: Flying's first implementation never gave them anywhere to land but the unit row. A Unit with Flying may now be played into an empty, unlocked, unreserved backrow zone of its controller's side as well as into empty unit zones, and one with Stack onto an occupied zone of either row; there it is a Unit for every rule and, like an animated card, no backrow card, attacked under [[R783002]] and [[R783003]], and in Defense Position it has landed. It takes R1081000, the first number of issue #1081's block. It edits [[§3.2]] and [[§6.1]].

**[[R1107000]] ends a watch with the match (issue #1107, patch v0.3.Y)**: a watcher was sent the finished match's last view and its socket then stayed open, in the players' count and reading Connected on the watch screen, until the reaper dropped the actor `REMATCH_OFFER_TTL_MS` later. Once a watcher has been sent the view that shows the result, its socket now closes with 1000 ("the match is over") and the players are told the new count; a watcher behind gets every view it is owed first, and a void still closes every watcher at once. It takes R1107000, the first number of issue #1107's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1108000]] keeps full art out of the tutorial (issue #1108, 2026-10-17)**: the heroes' cards print full art ([[R1017001]]), their whole text one hover away, which a new player meeting them in a lesson would have to find. A lesson's board now prints every card's words, the heroes' included, whatever the Full-art cards setting says; the setting, on by default, still rules every other screen. It takes R1108000, the first number of issue #1108's block. It edits [[§9.10]] and [[R1017001]].

**[[R1103000]] answers a watcher's later hello (issue #1103, patch v0.3.Y)**: the watch screen gained [[R1441]]'s wake probe, a hello sent to a socket quiet past the ping interval, but the actor read nothing a watcher sent after its first hello, so a live watch socket never answered the probe and every wake after a quiet stretch replaced it, a new sign-in and store check each time. The actor now answers a watcher's later hello with the view it was last sent, again, and nothing newer; a watcher sent no view yet is sent nothing, and every other frame is still dropped unread. It takes R1103000, the first number of issue #1103's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1115000]] reopens a match socket refused for an expired sign-in (issue #1115, patch v0.3.Y)**: a 1v1 or co-op socket closed with 4401 was never opened again, so a page that woke with an expired token before the gate's renewal came back ([[R1441]], [[R194]]) sent a player in a live match to the lobby. A 4401 is now final for the token it refused, not for the match: a different token opens exactly one more socket, the refused one opens none, and 4403 and 4404 stay final. It takes R1115000, the first number of issue #1115's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1127000]] ranks a Duos pass by the teammate's best next play (issue #1127, patch v0.3.X)**: best is the highest teammate follow-up score, the maximum of `evaluate` over the teammate's bounded legal `Play` follow-ups with the card in hand, scored from the teammate's seat; ties break by cost then hand order, and free-pass next-turn cards keep cost order with no lookahead. It takes R1127000, the first number of issue #1127's block. It edits [[§13.7]].

**[[R1141000]] says what a downloaded replay holds (issue #1141, patch v0.3.X)**: a player who saw something go wrong in a game had no way to hand a developer what their screen showed but a GIF of one turn. A row of the recent games that can be watched can now be saved as one JSON file: every step's view as the replay player is given it, the row's own facts and the build's catalog version, with no seed, log, state or deck, and no account's name or id, since a bug report may be public. It takes R1141000, the first number of issue #1141's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R1142000]] prunes the player statistics synced to the account to the public summary's inputs (issue #1142, patch v0.3.Y)**: the device keeps every counter ([[R639]]), but what syncs to the account is pruned to what the public player summary reads ([[R654]]): the game totals (`games`, `wins`, `losses`, `draws`) and, for each card, only `played`, `playedAgainst`, `destroyed` and `defeated`. Counters with a value of zero, all-zero card entries and `seen` (which the summary never reads) are left out. The server's body cap `PLAYER_STATS_BYTES_MAX` is 65,536 bytes, exported to `apps/web/src/wire/serverConfig.ts` so the client shares the bound. When a payload with all catalog entries and four-digit counters is pruned this way, it fits within the cap with room for future card sets. If a client's pruned payload exceeds the cap, `syncPayload` drops the lowest-count cards until the body fits, so the account copy keeps updating rather than failing silently on an oversized PUT. It amends [[R654]], takes R1142000, the first number of issue #1142's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R1006000]] to [[R1006004]] are the learned evaluation's data pipeline (issue #1006, part 1 of #466, 2026-10-16)**: the network #466 plans learns from positions, and the AI's records held none. Self-play now stores game logs, reproducible byte for byte from their seeds and never on a gate's or the dev set's deals ([[R1006001]], [[R1006002]]); a replay pass turns them into rows, dropping any game that no longer replays ([[R1006003]]), each row a seat's features read from its redacted view alone, the hand eval there and the game's outcome ([[R1006000]], [[R1006004]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1006's block.

**[[R1010000]] to [[R1010002]] put the learned evaluation in the search behind a switch (issue #1010, part 5 of #466, 2026-10-17)**: the network of part 4 scores the search's finalists after the opponent's reply when `AI_EVAL_KIND` says `Learned`, and nothing changes while it says `Hand` ([[R1010000]]); its inference is `f64` in one fixed order and matches the Python reference to the last bits ([[R1010001]]); a Duos duel keeps the hand eval ([[R1010002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1010's block.

**[[R1011000]] makes the learned evaluation shippable (issue #1011, part 6 of #466, 2026-10-17)**: an integer network, its first layer i16 over the raw features and its head i8, i64 sums, floor shifts and clips, gives the browser's WebAssembly build and the native gates the same answer bit for bit, which one committed fixture holds both to. It takes the first number of issue #1011's block.

**[[R1012000]] is the learn lane (issue #1012, part 7 of #466, 2026-10-17)**: the learned evaluation is retrained and promoted by a script, never an LLM, on the improve lane's bar and on hold-out deals of its own, so the AI has a supervised half (the improve and unban lanes) and a learned half that corrects it. The owner asked for the split. It takes the first number of issue #1012's block.

**[[R1012001]] takes the learned correction at a share (issue #1012, part 7 of #466, 2026-10-18)**: a finalist scores `hand + share·(points − hand)`, `LEARNED_CORRECTION_SHARE` of the network's correction. Fitted to outcomes, the whole correction judges positions a game apart better than lines a move apart: it changed about one decision in four for +0.010 edge, where half of it won +0.064 on the same 1,600 paired games.

**[[R1171000]] and [[R1171001]] add a learned policy prior to the beam (issue #1171, 2026-10-18)**: the beam expands only the first `root_branching` or `branching` of the heuristic's candidates, so a move ranked past them is never searched. A table fitted on the moves the AI played re-sorts each line's candidates before that prefix is taken, from keys that read only the seat's redacted view and name no instance ([[R1171000]]); it never adds, drops or changes a move, keeps End Turn, and stays off, with the null table, until a promotion turns it on with a fitted one ([[R1171001]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1171's block.

**[[R1136000]] lets "fill your board" fill a Locked unit zone (issue #1136, patch v0.3.Y)**: House Party cast after Showdown summoned one Right-house defender, into the lane Showdown left open, where the issue expected five. A Lock refuses only plays ([[R688]]) and a fill is a summon, but [[R64]] had kept a fill out of a Locked zone. "Fill your board" now summons into every empty, unreserved unit zone, left to right, a Locked one included; Meditative #26's backrow fill ([[R942]]) and Bear Honeypot's wait ([[R430]]) are unchanged. It takes R1136000, the first number of issue #1136's block. It edits [[§3.2]], [[§7]], [[§8.7]] and [[§8.8]], and amends [[R64]], [[R405]], [[R1080]], [[R1260]] and [[R1261]].

**[[R1144000]] keeps the last good sign-in keys across a failed refresh (issue #1144, patch v0.3.Y)**: every signed-in request verifies its token against the project's published key set, so once the cache passed its age a failed refetch failed every token with it, refetched on every request behind one mutex held across the fetch, and logged nothing. A failed refetch now keeps verifying against the last good set for at most `JWKS_STALE_MAX_MS` past its age limit (about one access-token lifetime), after [[R159]]'s rule that an unreachable provider keeps the identity; failures retry at most once per `JWKS_COOLDOWN_MS` with one fetch in flight at a time, and each is logged as `auth.jwks.fetch_failed` with its cause. A successful fetch replaces the set whole, so a key the endpoint dropped is refused at once, and past the cap the token is refused until the endpoint answers again. It takes R1144000, the first number of issue #1144's block, and cites [[§9.2]] and [[§9.4]].

**[[R1147000]] splits played card figures by the face the card was played on (issue #1147, patch v0.3.X)**: a card's figures split the decks that played it into two disjoint breakdowns, `playedRadiant` (the deck played the card Radiant at least once during the match) and `playedNormal` (the deck played it, but never Radiant), both counting decks and, over the records that keep a face for every play, adding up to `played`. Each seat in a game record holds `playedRadiant: Option<Vec<bool>>` recording whether the card was Radiant as played for every entry in `played`, in the identical order, tracked across hand transformations; records lacking a flag for some play count in `played` but in neither breakdown. It takes R1147000, the first number of issue #1147's block. It edits [[§9.11]], [[R376]] and [[R377]].

**[[R1156000]] gives both seats four opening cards (issue #1156, patch v0.3.Y)**: Player 1 drew 3 and Player 2 drew 4 plus The Coin, and for seat balance Player 1's opening draw is now 4 too: `OPENING_DRAW` is `[4, 4]`, still a table indexed by seat. Player 2 keeps The Coin ([[R244]]) and Player 1 the first turn; a handicap's extra cards still come on top ([[R182]]), and Quickdraw cards still replace draws out of the total ([[R640]]). Two decks that do nothing now fatigue out at player-turn 47 rather than 48, so the cap of 60 stays a backstop ([[R389]]). A game recorded before the change no longer reaches its recorded hash and is retired as `rules_changed` ([[R767000]]), which the owner accepted on the issue. It takes R1156000, the first number of issue #1156's block. It edits [[§2.1]], [[§2.5]] and [[§13.2]], and amends [[R182]], [[R389]] and [[R640]].

**[[R1177000]] and [[R1177001]] label every token as a token (issue #1177, patch v0.3.X)**: the designer asked that every token be labeled as one. The data already agreed, each token having `token: true`, rarity Token, the Token tag and its row in [[§7]], and a test now holds every set's tokens to the four ([[R1177001]]); the faces did not say it where a player meets a token. A full face's type line now reads "Token Unit", and a small face, a full-art face and the board minion wear a token mark ([[R1177001]]). A token fused with a real card kept the Token tag though it is a real card ([[R102]]), so it would have worn the label: it now sheds the tag ([[R1177000]], amending [[R102]]), which lets a fused token and card set off #85 like any real card. They take the first two numbers of issue #1177's block, and edit [[§5]], [[§7]], [[§8]] and [[§10.10]].
**[[R1175000]] names an embiggen card's two versions (issue #1175, part of #1173, patch v0.3.11)**: the designer asked that the cards with two prices be called embiggen cards, the cheaper price playing the normal version and the dearer one, the Embiggened cost, the Embiggened version. The eight embiggen cards' texts said "Paid (N): …" for the dearer version, R366's label, while the price picker captioned its cards Normal and Embiggened; every text and screen now says normal and Embiggened, and the label is "Embiggened (N): …". Wording only: no rule changes and the engine's identifiers keep their names. It takes R1175000, the first number of issue #1175's block. It edits [[§2.3]], [[§5]], [[§6.3]], [[§8.2]], [[§8.3]], [[§8.4]], [[§8.7]] and [[§8.8]], and amends [[R366]], [[R831013]] and [[R1144]].
**[[R1174000]] adds Frozen and Freeze (issue #1174, patch v0.3.X, part of #1173)**: the designer asked for Hearthstone's Freeze, a Unit that loses its next attack or change of battle position and unfreezes at the end of the turn in which it could have done it, for Meditative+ #24 Bob, Wisconsin's Strongest Soldier (#1185). Nothing froze before. Frozen is a status on the instance, not a keyword, with its thaw stamped as the freeze lands; while it is set the Unit has no exertion ([[§4.1]]), and every reader of the exertion agrees. Where the designer's words stop it follows Hearthstone: an effect's switch and a forced attack still happen, a Vanilla removes it as Silence does, a change of control keeps it, heroes are never Frozen. It takes R1174000, the first number of issue #1174's block. It edits [[§4.1]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§10.1]], [[§10.8]] and [[§12.3]], and amends [[R849000]], whose `attackReason` a Frozen unit now carries though it cannot act.
**[[R1178000]] to [[R1178002]] are the foundation of the Meditative+ set (issue #1178, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: the set is the second one [[R1420]]'s gate holds, in the catalog after Meditative and built card by card on `main` before its release lists it in `SHIPPED_SETS`, with its one new tag, Potion, waiting for the release's migration to join the server's tag check ([[R1178000]]); a pool may ask for the embiggen cards, the cards with two prices ([[R1178001]]); and the set's data the designer's list leaves open is decided: Dogpile for the second Pile On, Bob a Unit, Scratch Pad Mythic, its tokens and Bob's Plan's two AI cards ([[R1178002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1178's block; the batches take theirs from their own issues' blocks (`docs/meditative-plus-set.md` MP10). They edit [[§5]], [[§7]], [[§8]] and [[R1420]].

**[[R1168000]] to [[R1168003]] make a Duos game play the card base as a 1v1 game does (issue #1168, patch v0.3.X)**: three audits compared the catalog's cards in a co-op game with the same cards in a 1v1 game, on life, on turns and on passing, and found where [[§13]] was bypassed or silent. A passed card carried its memory into the teammate's duel, where its secret and journey ids named other things, so it keeps only what is the card itself ([[R1168000]]); a turn that ended as it began moved its duel two turns in one round, so a round's turn is the next one its duel began, and the view names each duel's round seat ([[R1168001]]); Glitch's reset, swap and void had no co-op reading, and a reset could freeze the round ([[R1168002]]); and three judges of lethal read own HP alone ([[R1168003]]). Meditative #19 Expedition123 and its Temporal Rift were checked end to end and needed nothing. They take the first numbers of issue #1168's block. They edit [[§13.1]], [[§13.3]], [[§13.4]], [[§13.5]], [[§13.6]] and [[§13.7]].
**[[R1179000]] to [[R1179004]] are Meditative+ batch 01, the cards on existing primitives (issue #1179, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: Spirit Lash's "all permanents" are every Unit on both sides, an animated card standing as a Unit included, one hit each before one state check, its Embiggened hits carrying Lifesteal and Pierce as stated and its Radiant Echo repeating at the version played ([[R1179000]]); Medina Grifter steals `cards` distinct random cards of the opponent's hand, hidden but for the count, a full hand burning the take and an empty one giving nothing ([[R1179001]]); Living Health Potion heals its controller's hero and each of their Units, the base face tributed first so it heals the others, the Radiant face healing itself too ([[R1179002]]); Potion cards are the non-token Potion cards of the sets that ship, M+ #11 to #13 under a preview, repeats allowed ([[R1179003]]); and Grubby Gloves takes the opponent's top `cards` cards with no random number, or `cards` random cards of their hand Embiggened ([[R1179004]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1179's block, need no engine change, and edit [[§8.9]] (#5, #10, #13, #14, #15, #19).
**[[R1182000]] to [[R1182002]] are the crafter's Meditative+ modes (issue #1182, B04 of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: Meditative #17's `craft` prompt gains a mode the prompt carries and the client reads. The potion frame of M+ #16 Jazakus is a Unit tagged Potion, of the Meditative+ set, whose one hat is "Activate: Tribute this.", at cost (1) and that cost's points, its own seeded Potions answering for the AI, the policy and a timeout ([[R1182000]]); M+ #16.1 Jazakus Prime's HUGE Potion is that frame at cost (3) with five times the points ([[R1182001]]); and Scratch Pad (M+ #26) opens #17's chain on an empty canvas with no presets, tooltips or hints shown, its presets kept for the AI, the card it makes the one #17 would ([[R1182002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1182's block (`docs/meditative-plus-set.md` MP10, which proposed them as MP-B04-1 to MP-B04-3). They edit [[§6.3]] (Craft), [[§7]], [[§8.9]] (#16, #16.1, #26), [[§10.6]], [[R882]] and [[R883]].
**[[R1180000]] to [[R1180005]] are Meditative+ batch 02, spell power (issue #1180, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: the brief's proposals MP-B02-1 to MP-B02-5 (`docs/meditative-plus-set.md`), decided where the designer's list leaves them open and Hearthstone semantics where it has them. A player's Spell Damage is one sum: Units, an aura for one player or both (M+ #9, [[R1180000]]) and "Spell Damage +N this turn" (M+ #11, [[R1180001]]); "your Spells have Lifesteal" is an aura over its controller's resolving Spells (M+ #7, [[R1180002]]), and its Radiant face's "twice as effective" healing doubles every heal of its controller's hero and Units after the replacements, two quadrupling as two Prophet Velens do ([[R1180003]]); health pays the next Spell's price, three times its cost, as Hearthstone's Bloodbloom, and may cost the game (M+ #6, [[R1180004]]); and the enemies a Spell targeted are kept with it so that one card in its player's hand or deck answers it, cast at the first still there (M+ #17 Dogpile, [[R1180005]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1180's block, and edit [[§4.4]], [[§6]] and [[§8.9]]. [[R1180006]] (issue #1185's integration, 2026-10-18) bounds the answer chain at `SPELL_ANSWER_CHAIN_CAP`, after the 200-seed fuzz found a deck that refilled itself with Dogpiles answering forever at an enemy that absorbed every hit.
**[[R1181000]] to [[R1181003]] are Meditative+ batch 03, the embiggen benders (issue #1181, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: the brief's proposals MP-B03-1 to MP-B03-3 (`docs/meditative-plus-set.md`), decided where the designer's list leaves them open and Hearthstone semantics where it has them. An effect "in N turns" waits through its owner's own turns started, an extra turn counting, and resolves at the start of the Nth before the draw, with a public countdown badge on its owner's side since a Spell is in no zone a mark could ride (M+ #4, [[R1181000]]); Jlockeed's Supply Drop's Combat hits each enemy once, Pierce and Lifesteal, its owner's Spell Damage raising each hit, and its Supplies adds Radiant non-token Jlockeed cards, never itself, the Radiant fill counted as it resolves ([[R1181001]]); Mountaintop Garden's aura reaches both players' embiggen cards, the normal price playing the Embiggened version and the dearer price, the normal price plus a premium, playing it Radiant too, while a Radiant card has the normal price only (M+ #22, [[R1181002]]); and Enormous Elderberry's "its Embiggened version costs (0)" is a price for that version alone, which the Garden's dearer choice reads and [[R766]] takes off (M+ #23, [[R1181003]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1181's block, and edit [[§6.2]], [[§6.3]], [[§8.9]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R1149000]] to [[R1149002]] are the designer's balance pass over 18 cards (issue #1149, patch v0.3.12)**: [[R1149000]] makes C #88 Siphon Squad inert until revealed: set and unrevealed, its aura takes nothing and its preview shows nothing, and once its own start of turn or anything else reveals it, it works as before; its self-Tribute stays live face-down and the Radiant's "0 Attack" stays last ([[R403]]). [[R1149001]] makes Meditative #37 CN in a Bottle a (1) Spell that casts on draw, adding a random Radiant card to its owner's hand, and one that reaches a hand without a draw is played for the same; it supersedes [[R926]], and [[R925]]'s hook stays for custom cards. [[R1149002]] named #13 Jlockeed Shredder-10, 6/10 → 8/20, as [[R275]]'s one exception to the stat half, until issue #1219 raised its Radiant Attack to 12 before the patch shipped: 6/10 → 12/20 doubles, so [[R275]] names no exception again. They take R1149000 to R1149002, the first numbers of issue #1149's block. They edit [[§8.1]], [[§8.6]], [[§8.8]], [[R275]], [[R403]], [[R602]], [[R767000]], [[R925]] and [[R926]].

**[[R1176000]] makes every card in Chinese CN (issue #1176, patch v0.3.X)**: the designer asked that all cards in Chinese gain the CN tribe. A card printed in Chinese already printed the tag; a card translated in a game (the `chinese` flag, [[R1300]]) had none, its flag read by no rule. Now an instance in Chinese has the CN tag wherever an instance's tags are read, as a granted tag does ([[R923]]): scopes, declarations, filters, plays counted by tag and the tribal-hate filters see it, the view lists it, and pools, which read printed tags, do not. A replay of a game before it whose Chinese cards were played or read by a tag misses its final hash and is refused ([[R767000]]). It takes R1176000, the first number of issue #1176's block. It edits [[§5.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§8.8]], [[§10.1]] and [[§10.8]], and amends [[R1300]].

**[[R1185000]] to [[R1185004]] are Bob and his plan (issue #1185, B07 of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: four systems Meditative+ #1 Bob's Plan, its AI generated cards #1.1 Base Camp and #1.2 Spa Day, #21 Harmony Spirit and #24 Bob, Wisconsin's Strongest Soldier are written with, each following Hearthstone where the spec was silent. Bob's Plan adds one random Wincon card and the AI card the brief chose as its plan, never a random one ([[R1185000]]); Base Camp's Ascent levels are [[R429]]'s play count raised on every copy its caster owns, in every zone, with nothing played ([[R1185001]]); Harmony Spirit's 3/3 is Hearthstone's set, layer 1 with the buffs and damage gone and the auras on top, and its Radiant Rush Tokens are summoned in Chinese ([[R1185002]]); Bob's second mode takes a Unit off the field into your hand as yours, as a Bounce takes it ([[R1185003]]); and Bob's four modes are declared, the take needing a target and the Coins its price, the Radiant face doing all four in order, the last a draw that fills the hand with copies ([[R1185004]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1185's block (`docs/meditative-plus-set.md` MP10, which proposed them as MP-B07-1 to MP-B07-4). They edit [[§6.3]] (Set stats, Steal, Draw, Raise a play count), [[§7]] and [[§8.9]] (#1, #1.1, #1.2, #21, #24).
**[[R1184000]] to [[R1184003]] are Meditative+ batch 06, KY's Competition (issue #1184, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: the brief's MP-B06-1 and MP-B06-2 (`docs/meditative-plus-set.md`, M+ #20 and #20.1), with what the build decided around them. M+ #20 asks C+ #42's `answer` prompt ([[R420]]) again after each right answer, a question rolled from the match rng each time, with Stop and the streak so far, the key never leaving the engine ([[R465]]), ending at a miss, a Stop or the 30th right answer ([[R1184000]]); a timeout stops it as it leaves a night market ([[R1184001]]); the practice AI works each question out from its statement, at once and with no search, so thirty questions stay inside its budget ([[R1184002]]); and `summon` takes an X, which M+ #20.1 Lil' Kenny's X/X and its keywords by X read ([[R1184003]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1184's block. They edit [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]], [[§7]], [[§8.9]], [[§9.9]], [[§10.2]] and [[§10.6]].
**[[R1194000]] lets Ivory Tower's Aura reach the Units in hand (issue #1194, patch v0.3.13)**: the designer buffed eight low-win-rate cards, and C+ #33 Ivory Tower gained "Aura: Your Units have Flying and Stack.". Both keywords change where a Unit may be played, which the spec read in the hand while auras reached the field; following [[R783007]], the Aura lends them to its controller's Units in hand and in the resolving zone as on the field, the Tower's own zone staying a carrier's. It takes R1194000, the first number of issue #1194's block. It edits [[§6.1]] and [[§8.7]], and amends [[R418]]; [[R1105]], [[R1106]] and [[R831012]] note the patch's Money Machine and Ever Growing Tree.

**[[R1183000]] to [[R1183005]] are the Meditative+ batch "outside data and the autopilot" (issue #1183, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: three cards read what lies outside a game's board — the compiled win rates, an AI, the cards' own code — and the brief's proposals MP-B05-1 to MP-B05-3 become rules. M+ #2 NA Tech reads [[R1100]]'s table from both ends, the lowest by the leader's floor and tie-breaks ([[R1183000]]), and fuses the two, different cards always, into a hand card at its own price ([[R1183001]]). M+ #18 Cognitive Offloading's "the AI plays the rest of your turn" is the autopilot, a status on its caster's own turn under which the engine plays for them ([[R1183002]]), by the §10.7 scorer's tries on their concealed board and within a bound of actions ([[R1183003]]); My Pawn's random turn ([[R44]]) was its model, Hearthstone having no such card. M+ #25 KY's Intuition's "blocks of card code" are each card's own script, compiled in by the cards' build ([[R1183004]]), and its Discover shows them as code alone ([[R1183005]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1183's block. They edit [[§6.3]], [[§8.9]], [[§10.3]], [[§10.7]] and [[§10.8]].
**[[R1186000]] is the AI with the Meditative+ set (issue #1186, part of #1173, 2026-10-18)**: [[R732000]] kept the shadow ban to the Meditative set, at most half of it, and the Meditative+ set joins it on the same terms, each set held to its own half (13 of its 26). Its sweep of record swept the Meditative+ cards alone, every set previewed as `fuzz` previews them ([[R1420]]), and `sweep --write` now reads the reports of record so that a card takes the row of the latest report that lists it, the Meditative bans standing beside the new set's rows. The sweep banned none of the 26: no game had an error or a timeout, and the five pass-1 `neverPlayed` flags all cleared in pass 2, so the AI deals itself the whole set and 11 of its cards are watched (`training/history/sweep-2026-10-18.md`). It takes R1186000, the first number of issue #1186's block. It edits [[R732000]].

**[[R1242000]] says what a downloaded co-op replay holds (issue #1242, patch v0.3.X)**: 1v1 replays can be downloaded and reopened ([[R1141000]]), but a co-op game had only Watch. A finished co-op replay now offers Download JSON, one `jackioh-duos-replay` v1 file of the seat's views with each step's index and round, the seat and the build's catalog version, with no seed, log, deck, match id, account or ping — though it carries the teammate's duel as the team shares it, so sharing the file shares that — and the recent games page opens such a file again on the co-op replay player. It takes R1242000, the first number of issue #1242's block. It edits [[§9.5]], [[§13.9]] and [[R1141000]].
