The Stinky Edition's Quests and Wincons are the Field Spells with either tag from every set ([[R1882001]]); its Acclaimed Unit refreshes its own cost once it is in the hand ([[R1882002]]); its "all enemies" are the enemy Units, switched to Attack Position and then Frozen ([[R1882003]]); and its rest-of-game Acclaimed Death is a death watcher on its caster, answering either player's Acclaimed deaths for the dead card's controller, at most three a player ([[R1882004]]). The Scape Edition's board swap draws from all ten Anomaly boards ([[R1882005]]); its two Systems are different ones cast in the order drawn ([[R1882006]]); "they have stack" tops each unit zone with a Drone Interceptor ([[R1882007]]); its launch fuses four pieces into its caster's Dormant Starship, a new one if none, and launches it for nothing ([[R1882008]]); and its Marketplace lot is its caster's alone, a Call to Chaos at a fixed 20 Credits ([[R1882009]]). They take R1882000 to R1882009, the first numbers of issue #1882's block, and edit [[§8.10]].

**[[R1884000]] to [[R1884002]] are the Stinkyscape set's release (issue #1884, part of #1856, 2026-10-27)**: the release lists the set in `SHIPPED_SETS` and owes the files of all 442 of its voice lines at once, which do not fit [[R1448]]'s 12 MiB beside the shipped sets' 887, so `VOICE_BUDGET_BYTES` is 16 MiB, still counted in 4 KiB blocks, every line still within 4 s ([[R1884000]]); and the release's fuzz wave found a would-die replacement that no firing spends asked again inside its own follow-up without end, so such a check no longer asks the card whose replacement is resolving ([[R1884001]]); and `classify`'s replay, which main's build cannot set up while it holds the set back, reads that refusal as the set's release, the major it already is ([[R1884002]]). They take the first numbers of issue #1884's block. They edit [[R501]] and [[R1448]]; [[R1884001]] adds to [[R1875006]] without changing it.

**[[R1885000]] admits Stinkyscape cards to the AI's shadow ban (issue #1885, part of #1836, 2026-10-28)**: the Stinkyscape set ships with the AI dealing itself none of it ([[R1390]]'s soft gate, the brief's ST5 D8), and the sweep of record its release starts ([[R1678000]]) could write no ban of it, since [[R732000]] and [[R1186000]] held the ban to the Meditative sets. Now the ban holds Stinkyscape cards too, at most `STINKYSCAPE_BAN_CEILING`, 35 of the set's 175 non-token cards (20%, ST6), the Meditative sets keeping their half, so that sweep writes its bans and, once it bans at most 35, the gate comes off. It takes R1885000, the first number of issue #1885's block. It edits [[R732000]], [[R1186000]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R2021000]] has a stand-down count only an enemy unit that could attack the unit on its next turn (issue #2021, 2026-10-27)**: [[R1759000]]'s stand-down asked only whether an enemy unit would kill the unit and survive the fight. So an enemy in Defense Position without Deft, a Frozen one, one that cannot attack, or a ground unit facing a Flying unit in Attack Position still made one, and each took one of the beam's two kept slots. An enemy now counts only when it is in Attack Position or has Deft in Defense Position ([[R49]]), may swing (`can_swing`, [[R1174000]]), and no attack restriction bars it from the unit ([[R783002]], [[R1839000]]). No constant or weight moved. It takes R2021000, the first number of issue #2021's block, and amends [[R1759000]].

**[[R2050000]] reads a `use` line outside a card script as code (issue #2050, 2026-10-27)**: `cargo jackioh classify` dropped every Rust file's `use` declarations along with its comments and tests, as `catalog loc` does for a card script. A pull request that only moved an import in the engine, the cards crate's shared code or the AI was therefore Internal, and CI's `rust (classify)` never asked the base build ([[R1365000]], [[R1370000]]). A card script still drops them and every other file keeps them. We rejected dropping them everywhere, since an import decides which code a call reaches. It takes R2050000, the first number of issue #2050's block, and refines [[R1365000]].

**[[R2067000]] is the most cast-on-draw cards one action casts (issue #2067, 2026-10-27)**: the daily fuzz's seed 9688 never ended an action, #21 Hinder's casts each discarding a card that Classic #64 Malzahar's Recycler answered with a draw, a chain of its own ([[R217]]), while #33 Unstable Clone Machine refilled the library with every cast, so [[R58]]'s cap ended each chain and never the action. One action now casts at most 60 cast-on-draw cards over all its chains, a library's worth, and each one drawn past that goes to the hand uncast as a chain's 21st does. It takes R2067000, the first number of issue #2067's block. It edits [[§2.4]].

**[[R2089000]] is the Created cards' own win rates (issue #2089, 2026-10-27)**: a game's record counted every card a seat played from its hand as its deck's and its figures walked the decklist alone, so a token was never counted and a Created copy of a deck card was counted as the deck's. The record now keeps a seat's dealt cards apart from the Created cards ([[R943]]) it made, credited to the controller of the card or effect that minted each, used or never used, each instance once; a Created card gets figures of its own, `createdPlayed`, `createdNotPlayed` and the used split by face, with a Created delta, which `stats-cards`, `cargo jackioh stats`, `GET /api/stats/cards` and `/stats` show apart from the deck table, with no migration and no back-fill. It amends [[R376]], [[R377]] and [[R1147000]], leaves CN Tech's and NA Tech's table the deck cards' ([[R1100]]), takes R2089000, the first number of issue #2089's block, and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2092000]] plays a co-op game again after it ends (issue #2092, 2026-10-27)**: either human seat of a finished co-op match offers Play again, and once both seats have offered the server starts one new co-op match for the same two people against the AI team: the host stays A1 at the same difficulty, under a fresh seed and the server's catalog. A saved deck is replayed from the finished match's `options`, a dealt one dealt afresh on its latest pick; the offers live in memory beside [[R672]]'s, an entry that made a game keeping its id so a late poller still finds it, and two offers racing make exactly one match. An offer past `REMATCH_OFFER_TTL_MS` from the end lapses silently and makes nothing; a stale catalog is `update_required`, a custom card in a frozen deck `loadout_invalid`, a seat in another game `already_in_match` with no details, as [[R1065000]] refuses a rematch, a blocked pair an unknown code, and a seat emptied by a deleted account a refusal. It takes R2092000, the first number of issue #2092's block, and edits [[§13.9]].

**[[R2094000]] is a co-op invite as a link, and a room link in chat as a Join button (issue #2094, 2026-10-27)**: the host shares `/together?coop=<CODE>` by chat or by hand instead of a bare code; R767's 1v1 link is unchanged. A valid code leaves the address bar for this tab at once and waits through a sign-in, kept as the code alone; an invalid one fills nothing. Play together then opens with the join form filled, Join focused, and the deck on the last co-op pick, else the first saved deck, else a random deal. The panel's room offers a Copy invite link, and its chat invite carries the link beside the code. A message holding this origin's `/play?room=` or `/together?coop=` link draws a Join button that navigates in-app, while the message stays text; another origin's link, or a malformed one, draws none. It takes R2094000, the first number of issue #2094's block, and edits [[§9.5]] and [[§13.9]].

**[[R2095000]] is the Both boards view of the Duos co-op board (issue #2095, 2026-10-27)**: on a screen wide enough for two boards, the player may show the teammate's duel beside their own, live, instead of flipping with the portal. The rail's "Both boards" toggle (`duos-both`, `aria-pressed`) keeps its choice per device in `localStorage`, starting off; the second board is the portal's read-only board (`legal` empty, a no-op `onAction`, `replay`) plus silence, so it feeds no audio; a hand card dragged onto it passes when `legal` lists that pass, as a drop on the portal does (issue #2619, 2026-10-31, removed both drags: a hand card dropped on the portal or the teammate's board passes nothing, and the inspected card's pass control is the only way to pass); the portal hides while both boards show and returns when the toggle is off, and below the phone breakpoint the toggle hides and the board falls back to one. It takes R2095000, the first number of issue #2095's block, and edits [[§13.6]].

**[[R2098000]] shows a glossary term's rule where a card's text prints it (issue #2098, 2026-10-27)**: a keyword in a card's text on the board, in the hand, in the Almanac's and the deck builder's pool and in the inspect views shows its glossary line in a tooltip on hover or a touch's long press, and on keyboard focus where the surface allows a control, while a tap still plays, aims, selects or opens the card. It takes R2098000, the first number of issue #2098's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2101000]] shows a ranked win streak as a flame (issue #2101, 2026-10-27)**: a flame with the count shows on the profile and each seat of a ranked match's line from `RANK_STREAK_FLAME_LENGTH` (3) on, and the match screen's ranks read carries both seats' streaks. It takes R2101000, the first of issue #2101's block, amends [[R612]] and edits [[§9.12]].

**[[R2102000]] lights a lethal hint for new players against the Easy practice AI (issue #2102, 2026-10-28)**: in a free game against the Easy AI, a sandbox game included, the board rings the enemy hero and End turn and says "You have lethal" while the human has a sure win this turn, never how. The worker asks the AI's lethal solver for the human's seat on the human's own redaction ([[R185]]), on worlds of its own stream, so the AI's hidden cards never change the answer and a sampled trap that stops the line keeps it dark; `decide` never asks it. It never shows at Medium or Hard, in a lesson, online or on a replay, waits for the board to show the newest view, and the Lethal hint setting (Gameplay, on by default) turns it off. No Hearthstone rule is involved: the hint changes no game. It takes R2102000, the first number of issue #2102's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2108000]] shows a menu's wait only once it outlasts a moment, and its empty panels as one empty state in the tavern's look (issue #2108, 2026-10-28)**: a skeleton or shell panel stays hidden until `SKELETON_DELAY_MS` (300 ms), a timer because Reduce motion zeroes CSS delays, and an empty panel draws a brass tankard beside its words with the next step where there is one. It covers the wallet, quests, packs, the workshop, Play, Replays, Play together, the patch notes and the statistics. SPEC §10 decided none of it. It takes R2108000, the first number of issue #2108's block.

**[[R2115000]] draws a skeleton where a menu panel waits on its read (issue #2115, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: a panel that printed "Loading …" while its data loaded draws a skeleton of a card grid, list rows, table rows or a generic panel instead, the sentence kept as a visually hidden `role="status"` line, the blocks `aria-hidden` and still under Reduce motion. It takes R2115000, the first number of issue #2115's block.

**[[R2116000]] fetches a screen's code, and a game screen's engine, on intent toward a control that leads there (issue #2116, part of #2036, 2026-10-27)**: a pointer, focus or touch on an in-app link or a navigating button prefetches that route's chunk once per page load, and the route renders from the same request with no loading panel. Prefetching every route when idle would spend metered data on screens never opened, and handlers on each of the roughly 45 navigating controls would scatter what one document listener does. It takes R2116000, the first number of issue #2116's block, and edits no section.

**[[R2119000]] makes four deletions undoable instead of asked (issue #2119, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: deleting a deck, a trio or a custom card, and removing a friend, take the item off the screen at once behind an undo toast for `UNDO_WINDOW_MS` (8 seconds), and the request waits for the window to close; it goes at once when another deletion takes the toast, when the page hides (as a `keepalive` request) or when the player leaves the screen, and in the workshop before anything that needs the item gone. Undo puts the item back and sends nothing; a held deck or trio is never saved meanwhile and no trio shows a held deck, whose slots come back on Undo. A request the server refuses puts the item back in its words; a deck or trio whose request could not reach the server stays deleted and is tried again ([[R256]]). Block and Concede still ask. SPEC §10 decided neither the window nor the held request, and Hearthstone has no undo here, so they follow common practice. It takes R2119000, the first number of issue #2119's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2120000]] shows a chat message at once while it sends (issue #2120, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: a message the player sends shows at the end of the conversation marked as sending, the draft clears, and sends go to the server one at a time in the order sent, so the server stores them in that order. The server's copy replaces the pending row when it answers, and while a send is open a read takes only the friend's messages, so a message shows once even when a poll lands before the answer. A refused or failed send is marked failed in the server's words, with Retry (the same body again, in its place) and Discard. Hearthstone's whispers say nothing of a send in flight, so this follows the common messaging pattern; parallel sends (the server may store them out of order) and matching a read's message to a pending one by its body (the word filter changes it, and two equal sends are ambiguous) are rejected. It takes R2120000, the first number of issue #2120's block. It edits [[§9.5]].

**[[R2121000]] lets a card or pile sheet be swiped away (issue #2121, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: on a touch screen a downward drag from a sheet's grip, a pile header or unscrolled content follows the finger and, past a distance or a flick velocity, closes through the same close as Close, while a prompt and the mulligan never close unanswered. Under reduced motion nothing follows and a swipe past the threshold closes at once. It takes R2121000, the first number of issue #2121's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2125000]] shows a menu's last answer at once and reads it again behind it (issue #2125, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: the collection, wallet, packs, cosmetic packs, tracks, quests, owned cosmetics, tokens, decks, ladder, one statistics filter's table and the friends list each keep their last answer in the tab's memory, keyed by path (query included) and account; a screen with one draws it at once without its skeleton ([[R2115000]]), reads again and takes the new answer, and a read that fails behind it keeps it and says nothing, so an error shows only on a first visit. Signing out and an account switch empty the cache; a buy, a pack opening, a chase, a token redemption, a deck or trio saved, deleted or imported and a friend accepted or removed update their entries from the answer; the decks workshop reopens when the decks that land differ from the kept ones. A match's, series', duos room's, watch's and replay's reads and anything that is not a `GET` are never kept. SPEC §10 decided none of it, and Hearthstone's collection and shop open on what the client last held. It takes R2125000, the first number of issue #2125's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2170000]] is the Stinkyscape set's own card art, its S27 (issue #2170, part of #1856, 2026-10-28)**: every Stinkyscape entry draws a space backdrop under a subject its family fixes, the Drone Cores and their drones one drone, the Starships and their pieces one fleet of warships, each Weapon the weapon its name says, and the set's other families each their own, both faces sharing their geometry ([[R2170000]]). It takes R2170000, the first number of issue #2170's block, and leaves [[R503]] as it is for every other set.

**[[R2173000]] and [[R2173001]] keep each shipped version's BUSTED and complicated lists and count a move across a threshold as a play change (issue #2173, part of #1856, 2026-10-28)**: what [[R1846003]] said the patch that ships the first card reading the lists owes. `patches ship` writes each version's lists, `card_metrics/<version>.json`, once its snapshot holds a card that reads them, and a fold under that version reads them, while a version with none reads the build's ([[R2173000]]). `classify` counts any change that moves a card a pool can draw across `BUSTED_CODE_LINES` or `COMPLICATED_CODE_LINES` as playing every shipped reader differently, so `--claims` asks a fragment for them ([[R2173001]]). Both are inert until the Stinkyscape release (#1884) ships #109 xxFreshJack and #111 Shockwave447, and no recorded game changes. They take R2173000 and R2173001, the first numbers of issue #2173's block, edit [[§10.7]], and amend [[R1846003]].

**[[R2176000]] and [[R2176001]] are the M12 audit's Stinkyscape fixes (issue #2176, part of #1856, 2026-10-28)**: the follow-ups of REVIEW Parts A and B on the held set ([[R1420]]). S #22 Gatinsir summons the Phalanxes by name, so each runs its Cry, as [[R411]] reads a summon that names its card; the set's other summons by name that reach a card with a Cry (S #6's Radiant S #7, the FO board's S #82 and S #83) already fire it ([[R2176000]]). S #150's death watch ([[R1882004]]) gets a stated terminator: it casts at most `CHAOS_STINKY_DEATH_WATCH_CASTS_PER_TURN` (5) Calls to Chaos for a player in a turn, each a chain with [[R28]]'s cap of its own ([[R2176001]]). The same issue corrected rulings it found reading otherwise than the engine plays: Shadow Word Death and Pain check their limit with the play and not again as they resolve, as No-No Words does ([[R1867005]], [[R1868000]]), and Jade Launcher's roll takes its controller's Luck ([[R1862005]], [[R987]]). They take R2176000 and R2176001, the first numbers of issue #2176's block, and edit [[§6.3]], [[§7]], [[§8.10]] and [[§10.1]].

**[[R2208000]] lets the AI of a practice game that earns finish each move (issue #2208, 2026-10-28)**: the browser cut every AI decision at `PRACTICE_AI_CLOCK_MS`, earning games included, while the server re-runs each AI move of a claim at the full budget with no clock ([[R1303002]]), so on a slow phone, or in a long game on any device, a won game could pay nothing, and its dialog blamed the AI's moves rather than the device. A game on a server seed now decides under `PRACTICE_EARNING_AI_CLOCK_MS`, many times that cap and only a backstop for a stuck worker, while the node budget stays the real bound; a free game without a server seed, a lesson and a sandbox game keep the short one. A decision that long cap still cuts marks the game, in its save too ([[R668]]), and the worker's claim answer carries the mark beside the claim, never in it. The claim is still sent, and a marked game's `ai_mismatch` refusal reads "this device ran out of time on one of the AI's moves"; every other refusal reads as before. Rejected: not claiming a marked game, since a cut move often matches the full one, and no cap at all, since a stuck worker would hold the game. It takes R2208000, the first number of issue #2208's block, and edits [[§9.9]], [[§14.8]] and [[R1303002]].

**[[R2211000]] draws a replay's own open prompt as it was offered, read-only (issue #2211, 2026-10-28)**: a replay's board drew no prompt, so the mulligan or a Discover passed with only the log's word that a choice was made, though each step's view ([[R768]]) carries the seat's own options. The board now draws them folded, greyed and inert, and the other seat's prompt as the waiting panel. Marking the option picked needs the next step's view. It takes R2211000, the first number of issue #2211's block, and edits [[§9.3]].

**[[R1884003]] to [[R1884005]] are the Stinkyscape release's fuzz fixes (issue #1884, part of #1856, 2026-10-28)**: the release's daily fuzz waves found games that never ended and a panic. A trap's text fused onto a Unit answered its own controller's Spells, which its condition never admits, because a fusion that is no trap queues its triggers like any card's and that path never read the condition; so a trap ingredient's trigger on a fusion that is no trap reads its condition before it runs, the case [[R2271000]] makes general ([[R1884003]]). And Job Application's Death brought back a Unit the board held at 0 max health, which died again at the same state check without end, so it brings back no Unit that died for want of health ([[R1884004]]). And a copier running The Maestro's text panicked checking its Activate's price, which the check read off the copier's own card, so it reads it as `param` does ([[R1884005]]). They take the next numbers of issue #1884's block, after [[R1884000]] to [[R1884002]].

**[[R2097000]] badges a deck holding a card the newest patch changed (issue #2097, 2026-10-27)**: while its latest save predates that patch and it holds cards the patch changed, its row in the deck workshop and its open editor wear "N cards changed in vX", which opens those cards with their changes; a flavor-only change is none, and a save under the patch clears it. It takes R2097000, the first number of issue #2097's block. It edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2100000]] keeps a saved deck's earlier versions, and restores one (issue #2100, 2026-10-27)**: a player who tweaked a deck and regretted it had no way back. Each save now keeps a version of the deck's name and cards on the account, a burst of saves within `DECK_VERSION_DEBOUNCE_MS` (60 s) of the newest being one version and a save that changes neither keeping none, at most `DECK_VERSIONS_KEPT` (20) per deck. The deck builder's History panel lists them newest first with the cards in and out of each, and a restore saves one as the deck's, checked as a save is, and as a new version, so the one it replaced stays. Hearthstone keeps no deck history, so the numbers are the issue's planner's, each one constant. It takes R2100000, the first number of issue #2100's block, and edits [[§9.4]].

**[[R2111000]] gives a trap's set, wait and flip their own motions (issue #2111, 2026-10-29)**: a backrow arrival slammed like a unit, a face-down card stood still and a trap turned at once. Now a backrow arrival is laid down where its thud sounds, every face-down card breathes one breath from one rule, and a firing trap holds a beat of anticipation before it turns at 30%, where the burst now lands. Reduced motion stops all three; the sound is [[R203]]'s. It takes R2111000, the first number of issue #2111's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2117000]] cross-fades a move between two menu screens in the browser's view transition (issue #2117, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: menus swapped in a single frame. Now a link, a redirect or Back/Forward between two menus runs inside `document.startViewTransition` with the default fade. A board's route never fades, entered or left, and neither does a board on screen (a replay in place among them). A change of query alone does not fade, nor does any move under Reduce motion or without the API, and a move the leave guard holds ([[R1754000]]) waits until it goes. Knowing a board by its route alone was rejected. It takes R2117000, the first number of issue #2117's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2118000]] is the command menu (issue #2118, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: Ctrl+K or ⌘K opens it on every menu screen and never over a board, with New deck, Practice, Play, the pages and the Almanac's shelf by name ([[R630]], [[R1420]]), each a move the leave guard holds ([[R1754000]]). It takes R2118000, the first number of issue #2118's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2122000]] gives every control on a phone a 44x44 px tap area (issue #2122, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: under `(pointer: coarse), (max-width: 600px)`, a control shorter than `--hit-min` takes the tap through a transparent centered `::before` from `index.css` (one as tall but narrower through its own stylesheet's), a small input or select through its own height, and a checkbox or radio through its labels, while neighbours split the gap so no hit area covers another control. Inline links, cards and controls under an open modal are exempt, and spec 47 measures the board and every menu of [[R1581000]] at 390x844 on every pull request. It takes R2122000, the first number of issue #2122's block.

**[[R2124000]] runs a retried spend once (issue #2124, part of #2036, 2026-10-28)**: a spend the server finished after the client gave up on it (`API_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) ran again when the player pressed again. Every spend now takes an optional `Idempotency-Key`, a UUID the client mints for a press and sends again on that press's retries; under the account's economy lock the same key with the same route and body replays the answer it committed, another route or body is 422 `idempotency_key_reused`, and an unknown key's answer is written with its grant, kept `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_HOURS` and swept. It takes R2124000, the first of issue #2124's block, and edits [[§14.11]].

**[[R2126000]] shows the client's own keyboard shortcuts (issue #2126, part of #2036, 2026-10-29)**: ⌘ or Ctrl by platform, each shortcut in its control's tooltip and aria-keyshortcuts, the command menu's Enter and keys and a footer line for Ctrl/Cmd+K, none on a touch-only device and no new key handler on a board. It takes R2126000, the first number of issue #2126's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2179000]] keeps an Indestructible unit out of Defense Position (issue #2179, 2026-10-28)**: Indestructible also carries "Cannot be in Defense Position", so an Indestructible unit is never switched to Defense and one standing in it switches to Attack at the state check, as Warp Disruptor's aura makes every Unit do ([[R1849006]]); it never has Taunt, as [[R347]] already read. A turn whose only action left was that switch ends itself ([[R82]]). It takes R2179000, the first number of issue #2179's block, and edits [[§4.1]], [[§4.5]] and [[§6.1]].

**[[R2179001]] gives Temporary a number (issue #2179, 2026-10-28)**: Temporary X counts down by 1 at each cleanup of its owner's turn and is discarded at 0, a plain Temporary being Temporary 1 as before ([[R637]]); the count left rides the card and is the one it shows. C+ #23 Dropshipping gives Temporary 2 where it gave Brittle 2. It takes R2179001 and edits [[§6.1]].

**[[R2179002]] makes a cast on draw no play (issue #2179, 2026-10-28)**: a card cast on draw is still a free cast through [[§10.5]]'s steps, but nothing counts it as a play (Combo, the turn log, the game counters, the last Spell) and no card answering a play answers it: its `cardPlayed` wakes no trigger, and its `cardResolved`, which still reaches every card, carries `onDraw` for the cards that answer plays to pass by. An Echo's repeat was never a play, which a test now pins. [[R155]]'s return flag is cleared from the graveyard, since the turn log no longer holds such a card. It amends [[R40]] and [[R70]], takes R2179002 and edits [[§6.2]], [[§6.3]] and [[§10.5]].

**[[R2179003]] rewrites KY's Test's problems (issue #2179, 2026-10-28)**: an Easy problem is a four-digit sum or difference with its three wrong answers a slip of ±1, ±10 or ±100, the old Hard problems are the Medium ones, and the Hard ones are new, at a doctoral level. It amends [[R420]] and [[R580]] and takes R2179003.

**[[R2179004]] makes Playtester's Book random (issue #2179, 2026-10-28)**: Meditative #85 Playtester adds a Book of Buff or a Book of Nerf by one pick of the match rng, with no prompt, where [[R1161]] asked its controller. It takes R2179004.

**[[R2179005]] lets a per-hit cap guard its own unit (issue #2179, 2026-10-28)**: C+ #11 Anime Armor's cap now holds on the unit itself as on its controller's hero (`guardsSelf`), at [[§4.4]] step 3 after Armor, and nothing tramples past a unit the capped hit leaves standing. It takes R2179005.

**Issue #2179's numbers (2026-10-28)**, the Meditative balance changes IV: the other cards it changed are catalog data or kept scripts ([[R767000]]) and take no ruling: Tranquility exiles itself; Knowledge Breaker no longer reaches decks; Felinor Fuser is 3/3 → 6/6 and Discovers (2) or (3) Cost or less; Transmogulate is (2) embiggen (4), its normal version reaching hand and deck and its Embiggened one the board too; Conjure Bones' Embiggened count is 14; Devil's Pact's Radiant face makes a base Book of Flame; Book of Nerf and Joon Jorker are 4 times (Radiant 8); Catboy Maid SSR+ adds 1-2 (Radiant 1-4); State of the Game is Immutable; Magic Amplifier is (1) embiggen (2), its Embiggened version adding a damaging Spell or Field Spell; Prep is (0) embiggen (2), its Embiggened version drawing your highest cost Spell ([[R24]]); Anime Armor is 3/3 → 6/6; Otherworldly Removal costs (3); Wondrous Wand's cards cost (1) (Radiant (0), its Embiggened draw 4); Trenful Trickster is 1/4 → 2/8; Blood Moon costs (2); The Power to Punish costs (3) and its damage is Pierce; Spiritually 中国 costs (4) and does all three of its effects, its Radiant face printed in English ([[R920]]'s roll is the replays' since); Radiant Saintess costs (2), its Radiant face Reborn and radiating no hand; Efficiency Dividend heals 3X (Radiant 6X); Medina Grifter is 3/1 → 6/2; Book of Buff's base face no longer reaches a hand; and Claude's Datacenter is an Activate. The rows are in [[§8]].

**[[R2194000]] to [[R2194002]] are the Training Grounds foundation (issue #2194, part 1 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the owner asked, on 2026-10-28, for a new set, Training Grounds, in the normal game, and for the tutorial to be remade from it. The set is built on `main` behind [[R1420]]'s gate as the fourth held set, after Stinkyscape in the catalog order, until its release (#2204) ships it as the newest set ([[R2194000]]). The designer's "Training-grounds" and "Tutorial-Grounds" are the set, and a pool that names it works while it is held ([[R2194001]]). Its data: #1–#40 and the token #39.1 by the designer's numbers and rarity bands, the twenty Core snapshots #41–#60 at their Core rarities, #28 and #29 read as Units, and the designer's names as written ([[R2194002]]). `docs/training-grounds-set.md` reads every card against the engine and plans the parts that build them.

**[[R2195000]] to [[R2195003]] are the Training Grounds snapshots (issue #2195, part 2 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the designer asked for twenty Core cards to be snapshotted into the set as #41–#60, "separate entities", and the owner decided on 2026-10-28 that a snapshot keeps its Core card's name and that a deck holds one of the two. Each snapshot names its Core card (`snapshotOf`) and a deck, a random deck and the deck builder take one of the pair, never both ([[R2195000]]); the pair alone may share a name ([[R2195001]]); a text naming the Core card names it alone ([[R2195002]]); and each is a frozen copy that plays as its Core card did at the copy, what other cards read off its own number and code excepted ([[R2195003]]).

**[[R2196000]] to [[R2196003]] are the Training Grounds units on existing keywords and Cries (issue #2196, part 3 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the part replaces the foundation's stubs for #1–#8, #16, #17, #22, #23, #28, #29, #33 and the token #39.1 with their scripts, each a shipped card's pattern, and settles the four readings the brief left to it. Personal Trainer's Radiant "a friendly Human" is the base face's "another", the pick being made in hand ([[R2196000]]). Celestial Caller's left and right are the zones one lane lower and higher on its side, an edge or a taken zone making that one summon fail ([[R2196001]]). Right-Hand Felinor is to the right of the Unit acting one lane lower on its own side and row ([[R2196002]]). Occidental's rightmost lane is the last and Oriental's leftmost is lane 1, both sides and both rows, the base face destroying the card itself when it stands there ([[R2196003]]).

**[[R2197000]] to [[R2197003]] are Training Grounds' triggers, adjacency aura and Trigor (issue #2197, part 4 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: building TG #9, #18, #21, #25, #30, #32, #36 and #38 from `docs/training-grounds-set.md` TG5. Knife Juggler's "after you summon a Unit" is any Unit arriving on its controller's side as [[R1875003]] reads an arrival, its own excepted ([[R2197000]]). Flame Tongue Totem's aura reaches the top Unit in each zone beside it, on its side and row, the first adjacency aura ([[R2197001]]). Trigor's successor is cumulative, each one's base stats the dying one's plus the growth, the terminator REVIEW A3.6 asks for (the brief's TG-P4-3, [[R2197002]]). Floodgates counts arrivals on either side and its Death destroys its controller's Units ([[R2197003]]).

**[[R2197004]] is Trigor's terminator (issue #2197, 2026-10-29)**: the handicap fuzz on `main` found two Trigor chains that never ended (seeds 1543 and 9463: Warp Disruptor II forcing a Poisonous Unit, or the other side's Trigor, into each new Trigor), so [[R2197002]]'s growth was no terminator. A Trigor that a Trigor's Death summoned this turn now summons nothing when it dies this turn ([[R2197004]]).

**[[R2197005]] is Floodgates' terminator (issue #2197, 2026-10-29)**: the AI's sweep found a fused Unit carrying TG #30 Floodgates' Death and Stinkyscape #112 Job Application's self-resummon dying forever (`sweep:hard:traininggrounds-014:6`). Floodgates' Death now spares the body it dies on, and on a fused or granted body that arrived this turn destroys nothing ([[R2197005]]).

**[[R2198000]] to [[R2198004]] are the Training Grounds spells (issue #2198, part 5 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the systems four of the set's Spells are written with, each following Hearthstone where the spec was silent (`docs/training-grounds-set.md` TG4, which proposed them as TG-P5-1 to TG-P5-4). Dino-size's set is [[R1185002]]'s on one Unit (`set_stats`, [[R2198000]]); Seance's exact copy is [[R57]]'s copy of a card on the field with its price, a stated price replacing it (`add_to_hand`'s `exact`, [[R2198001]]); In a hurry's tokens are destroyed at the end of the turn they arrive, whoever's, a stolen one included, and print no Brittle ([[R2198002]]); and a Spell's Cleave on a board pick reaches the permanents beside it in its row and side, the board twin of [[R1563001]] ([[R2198003]]). Fortify keeps its name: "Fortify Mind" joins [[R961]]'s rules phrases, still naming M #22.1 itself ([[R2198004]]). They edit [[§6.3]] (Set stats, Add to hand, Destroy) and [[§8.11]] (#15, #26, #27, #34, #37).

**[[R2199000]] to [[R2199007]] are Training Grounds' backrow part (issue #2199, part 6 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the scripts of the set's Traps, Field Spells and Field Trap, TG #13, #14, #19, #20, #24, #31 and #35, each proved in its own card file, and the one engine piece they lacked, proved in the engine's rules tests too. "After one of your Units attacks" is a new hook, `allyAttack`, run for the attacker's side once the combat's state check has closed, after the combatants' own hooks ([[R2199000]]). A Trap's printed Pierce and Trample are stated on its own hits ([[R2199001]]). Explosive Runes springs on an enemy Unit's declared attack on your hero and hits every enemy, so an attacker it kills fights no combat ([[R2199002]]); Explosive Trap hits a played Unit still on the field after it resolves ([[R2199003]]). Recruitment Posters never adds itself ([[R2199004]]). Goliath Situation fires on every Unit the opponent plays, at the cost paid ([[R2199005]]). Corpse Recycler answers both sides' non-Token Units ([[R2199006]]), and Level Up! counts each Unit's attacks apart from its arrival ([[R2199007]]). Where the designer said nothing, Hearthstone's semantics decide; each default is the brief's (`docs/training-grounds-set.md` TG6).

**[[R2200000]] to [[R2200004]] are Training Grounds #39 Call to Chaos (Training-Grounds Edition) (issue #2200, part 7 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the set's Legendary Call to Chaos, held with its set ([[R1420]]), is built on Core #95's roller with a table of its own. Its "Training Grounds cards" and "permanents" are the set's non-token cards ([[R2200000]]). Its first entry's Taunt and Divine Shield reach the tokens it summons, and its "refresh 4 mana" is §6.3 Refresh ([[R2200001]]). Its "every other turn" is a new rest-of-game effect: one Call to Chaos now, then one at the start of every second turn of the caster's, capped at 3 a player ([[R2200002]]). Its Rush, Trample and Pierce are three player modifiers ([[R2200003]]). It joins "a random Call to Chaos" only when its set ships, and The Big Crunch's list with the release ([[R2200004]]). The designer's "every other turn" and "a Call to Chaos" take the brief's defaults (TG6 row 25).

**[[R2201000]] and [[R2201001]] are the rules a tutorial lesson stands on (issue #2201, part 8 of #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the owner asked that the tutorial be unlosable and that its Legendary and Mythic cards not show up until the last lesson, both as rules of the game a lesson runs. A handicap's `heroFloor` is the least health that seat's hero can fall to, held against every way to lose: each hit capped at what the hero has above it, life loss and set health stopping there, and no win of the opponent's read against it, while the seat still wins as ever; `TUTORIAL_HUMAN` sets it to 1, which makes today's four lessons unlosable at once ([[R2201000]]). A handicap's `poolHold` keeps Legendary and Mythic cards out of every random pool its game draws from, read through a thread-local each engine step sets for its one call, so it travels in a lesson's handicaps and reaches no other game ([[R2201001]]). [[R1012003]]'s tutorial opponent is now read by its resources, so neither field moves it off the hand eval. They amend [[R290]], [[R1705005]] and [[R1012003]] and edit [[§2.5]], [[§5.1]] and [[§9.10]]; the remade tutorial (issue #2205) sets the pool hold on its first four lessons.

**[[R2202000]] and [[R2202001]] are the Training Grounds set's look and sound (issue #2202, part 9 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: as Stinkyscape's set had its own art ([[R2170000]]) and its mark and intros ([[R1883000]]) before its release, the held Training Grounds set ([[R2194000]]) draws its own art, a training yard under one subject per family, its snapshots #41–#60 keeping their Core cards' pictures ([[R2202000]]), and wears a mark of its own, a straw target over crossed wooden swords, with intros for its five Legendaries, Mythic and token printed Legendary ([[R2202001]]). Presentation only; they take the first two numbers of issue #2202's block.

**[[R2203000]] admits Training Grounds cards to the AI's shadow ban (issue #2203, part 10 of #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the owner asked for the Training Grounds set in "the normal game too", so it ships with the AI dealing itself its cards, not behind [[R1390]]'s soft gate as Stinkyscape did. The sweep of record over the previewed set ([[R1420]]) could write no ban of it, since [[R732000]], [[R1186000]] and [[R1885000]] held the ban to the Meditative and Stinkyscape sets. Now the ban holds Training Grounds cards too, at most half the set's non-token cards as the Meditative sets', so that sweep writes its bans before the release. It takes R2203000, the first number of issue #2203's block. It edits [[R732000]], [[R1885000]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R2203001]] has the AI answer every Training Grounds prompt through its search, and try a midlane play apart from its neighbours (issue #2203, 2026-10-28)**: the set's targets, lanes, face-down sets and Call to Chaos rolls are all plays `legal_actions` lists, so the search, the random policy and a timeout answer them with nothing new. But the search's root folded lanes that read alike around them into one placement (#1014, #1635), so on an empty board the midlane play of TG #5 Midlane Menace (and of Classic #22 Mid Runner) was never tried. A placement that holds a midlane lane ([[R685]]) is now tried there, its lowest lane otherwise, so the search branches no wider. It takes R2203001.

**[[R2204000]] and [[R2204001]] are the Training Grounds release (issue #2204, part 11 of the tracker #2193, 2026-10-28)**: the set ships as the newest set ([[R1371]]), and the owner decided on 2026-10-28 that it is wrapped into Stinkyscape's v0.5 line rather than opening a line of its own, so a set the owner wraps ships as a patch of the current line, by its cards' tier, and renames the line ([[R2204000]]). With the snapshots in every pool that names no set, a pool keeps both cards of a pair and one offer shows at most one of them ([[R2204001]]), the default the issue's comments named.

**[[R2204002]] closes a gap the release found in the lesson hold (issue #2204, 2026-10-29)**: with the Training Grounds cards in its random decks, the hold's sweep dealt a Scanning Probe whose Anomaly laid Monument's Sanctum, a Legendary, in a held game, so a held game draws its Anomaly from the boards that lay no held card ([[R2204002]]), as [[R2201001]]'s weighted tables leave a held entry out.

**[[R2210000]] is the daily quest replace (issue #2210, 2026-10-28)**: a quest the player did not want held its slot until they finished it, and the full slots skipped every arrival, so each held quest may now be replaced `QUEST_REPLACES_PER_DAY` times a UTC day, through `POST /api/quests/:questId/replace`, by a quest drawn as an arrival is, neither held nor the one replaced, with its progress lost and the arrival rhythm unmoved; `GET /api/quests` and the replace answer `replacesLeft`, and the quests panel shows Replace on each quest while one is left, behind a confirm. It takes R2210000, the first of issue #2210's block, adds `QUEST_REPLACES_PER_DAY` to `crates/server/src/config.rs` and `serverConfig.ts`, and edits [[§14.9]] and [[§14.11]].

**[[R2214000]] A ladder bot's missed decision is a beginner's move, not a uniform one (issue #2214, 2026-10-28)**: a decision a ladder bot's skill does not give to the AI ([[R747000]]) is no longer [[§10.7]]'s uniform random move but a softmax draw, on the bot's own stream, over the greedy baseline's one-ply scores of its legal moves on one world dealt from what it sees, so a weak bot ends a turn with a card it could play, or makes an attack that loses it value, far less often. The strengths were calibrated again with it ([[R748000]]): they still stand apart, from 1517 for the weakest to 1832, and only new bots take the new seeds.

**[[R2246000]] A Frozen card can't use its Activate (issue #2246, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. Frozen ([[§6.1]], [[R1174000]]) stopped a Unit's attack and its switch, and an Activate, which no exertion binds ([[R384]]), still fired, so a Unit Frozen forever by Stinkyscape #2 Absolute Zero kept its weapon. Now the Activate refusal reads the status: a Frozen card's abilities are neither listed nor accepted until it thaws, its uses untouched, while another card's effect may still run them ([[R1876001]]). It takes R2246000, the first number of issue #2246's block, and edits [[§6.1]] Frozen and [[§6.2]] Activate.

**[[R2247000]] Anomaly Found! shows the Anomaly it holds (issue #2247, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. [[R1879000]] drew the board as the Spell resolved, so the card could not say which it was. Now it draws as it arrives, as Heroic Power rolls its power ([[R43]]), and remembers the code as a label its owner's view carries (`CardView.label`) and the client prints in place of its text, "FO" or "AN"; its Cry lays that board, and one that holds none still draws as it resolves. It takes R2247000, the first number of issue #2247's block, and edits [[§7]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R2248000]] A Flying Unit summoned into a full unit row stands in the backrow (issue #2248, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. [[R1081000]] let a Flying Unit be played into the backrow but no summon put one there, so a drone summoned into a full unit row fizzled. Now a summon that names no lane puts a Flying Unit in the leftmost open backrow zone when its unit row has none, standing there as a played one does; a named lane, "fill your board" and a layered summon are as before. It takes R2248000, the first number of issue #2248's block, and edits [[§3.2]] and [[§6.1]] Flying. On 2026-10-30 (issue #2453) the owner asked that every summon of a Flying Unit may land in the backrow, as Drone Core VIII's "fill your board" did not: a fill with a Flying Unit now fills the backrow after the unit row, and the Recruit of every permanent, M #63's summon from hand and the attack summons ([[R1202]], [[R1203]]), which looked only at the unit row before they drew or moved, read a Flying Unit's backrow too; a named lane, a layered summon and a random fill are as before. It edits [[R2248000]], [[R1860008]], [[§3.2]], [[§6.1]], [[§6.3]] and [[§7]].

**[[R2249000]] Starships arrive in the middle of the backrow, list their pieces and keep their art (issue #2249, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. [[R1852000]] summoned a Dormant Starship into its owner's leftmost open unit zone; it now goes to the middle of their backrow first (lanes 3, 2, 4, 1, 5), standing there as a Flying Unit stands ([[R1081000]]), then the unit row in that order, then on top of lane 3 as before. Its printed text is cut to a line and its Activate, the rest left to the Starship and Launch terms ([[R1859013]]), and in play its face prints no text, only its pieces as card references, under art of its own that a fusion does not change. It takes R2249000, the first number of issue #2249's block, and edits [[§3.2]], [[§6.1]] and [[§8.10]].

**[[R2250000]] to [[R2250002]] put Resources in the hand and give the Marketplace three offers a turn (issue #2250, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. [[R1840000]]'s Resource tray, a second hand no hand rule reached, is gone: a gained Resource is added to the hand, taking a slot and burning when the hand is full, and is played and discarded as any hand card is ([[R2250000]]). Played or discarded, it pays its player its quote in Credits ([[R2250001]]), so the Marketplace no longer buys or sells Resources. The Marketplace's one offering of every lot, restocked every six turns ([[R1853001]]), becomes three offers rolled for each player at the start of their turn from the table's lots less the Resources, each lot as likely as its count, one copy each ([[R2250002]]). They take R2250000 to R2250002, the first numbers of issue #2250's block, and edit [[§3]], [[§6.3]] and [[§10.8]].

**[[R2255000]] shows this season's ranked record on the profile (issue #2255, 2026-10-28)**: the account screen's rank block reads "This season (ranked): W–L–D" from `GET /api/ranked`'s `record`, left out while the season has no games and when the rank cannot be read, while the all-time Record section stays as it was. It takes R2255000, the first number of issue #2255's block, and edits [[§9.12]].

**[[R2259000]] A Dormant card that awakens is summoning sick (issue #2259, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. [[R1839002]] had an awakened card not summoning sick, as Hearthstone's awakened minions are not, so a Dormant Starship launched for (3) by its Activate, or an Imprisoned Unit whose countdown ended at its controller's turn start, attacked at once. Now an awakening, however it comes, makes the card summoning sick for the rest of that turn, its `summonedTurn` the turn it awoke in: Rush and Charge let it attack as they let any sick Unit, and the invariant monitor's shadow counts the awakening as an entry. It takes R2259000, the first number of issue #2259's block, and edits [[§6.3]] Awaken and [[§8.10]] #17.

**[[R2260000]] A Dormant card hosts no Equip play (issue #2260, 2026-10-28)**: the owner's first pass over the Stinkyscape set. A Dormant card is out of play and nothing reaches it ([[R1839000]]), yet `equip_host_at` took any Unit its controller acts with on top of a zone, so an Equip card could be played onto a Dormant card, the Dormant Starship among them, and fuse into it. Now a Dormant host is no host: no Equip play onto its zone is offered or accepted until it awakens, while a Stack play onto it stays as [[R1839003]] and [[R1852007]] have it. It takes R2260000, the first number of issue #2260's block, and edits [[§6.1]] Equip.

**[[R2261000]] and [[R2261001]] take what a card on the field has spent off a Nerf's and a Buff's menu (issue #2261, 2026-10-28)**: [[R1748000]] had taken off the paid cost and the numbers only a Cry reads. A Tribute Unit in play could still have a Buff spent lowering the Tribute it had paid, and a number one face's Cry reads, while the other face's lasting text read it too, went unmarked, so a base M #101 Gachaholic in play could still have a Buff spent on its resolved Cry's Lucky. On the field the X row now never draws Tribute ([[R2261000]]). A number only one face's Cry reads is marked `cry: "base"` or `"radiant"`, and on the field it is out of reach while the card shows that face ([[R2261001]]). Building it showed that a play read the printed Tribute, though [[R386]] has a Nerf or a Buff move it: a Buffed card in hand showed Tribute 1 and still asked for 2. It now asks for the Tribute the Nerf or the Buff left. Both changes hold for a Nerf as for a Buff, since the two share one menu, and KY's Constant, no Nerf or Buff, still reaches both numbers. They take the first two numbers of issue #2261's block and edit [[§5]], [[§6.3]], [[§8]], [[R386]] and [[R1748000]].

**[[R2271000]] and [[R2271001]] are issue #2271's (part of #1836, 2026-10-28)**: the M12 audit's final pass found that a trigger's condition was read only by the traps' dispatch, so a card that is no trap and printed its condition only there answered every event its trigger names, Meditative #97.8 Prison rolling to capture its own controller's plays, and an awake Stinkyscape #17 Imprisoned Ship Smith or #19 Imprisoned Cosmality answering every other card's awakening, among them; every queued trigger now reads its condition as it resolves, and a trap's is still read once ([[R2271000]]). And a copier that copied a fused card asked none of the targeting cost the fused card asks, so it now asks the stricter of its ingredients' ([[R2271001]]). They take the first numbers of issue #2271's block. They extend [[R99]], [[R102]] and [[R1850000]].

**[[R2285000]] to [[R2285002]] are the two attack timings and the Classic Golem they bring back to its design (issue #2285, 2026-10-28)**: the owner asked, on 2026-10-28, to codify that "When this attacks" resolves before the attack happens, before any damage, and "After this attacks" after it, only if the attacker is still alive. [[R2285000]] makes "When" and "Whenever this attacks" the attacker's `whenAttacks` hook, run after a declared attack's trap window and before a forced one's combat, so Classic #45 Nature Titan draws and heals and Stinkyscape #75 Badger and #76 Honey Badger gain before the damage, where they had resolved after it. [[R2285001]] runs `afterAttack` only for an attacker still on the stay it attacked from, so #32 Prem Panther (reversing balance patch 1's [[R426]] reading, which the owner confirmed), Classic #13 Boots on the Ground and Stinkyscape #91 and #92 do nothing after an attack they died in. [[R2285002]] has Classic+ #73.1 Classic Golem transform the Unit it attacks, before the damage, as the designer's note says, the base Golem into a Radiant Unit and the Radiant Golem into a base-face one, the attack going on against the new Unit, and lets a Golem that destroys it attack again; it replaces [[R424]]. They take R2285000 to R2285002, the first numbers of issue #2285's block.

**[[R2291000]] lights the lethal hint in every offline game against the AI (issue #2291, 2026-10-28)**: [[R2102000]] kept the hint to a free game against the Easy AI. Now the practice worker asks it with every snapshot of every game it plays: Easy, Medium and Hard, a sandbox game, a game with custom cards, Try it and a URL seed, and a tutorial lesson. It still never shows online (a Duos game against the AI team included), in hotseat or on a replay. The check is unchanged, and the Lethal hint setting still turns it off. Keeping it to Easy was rejected. It takes R2291000, the first number of issue #2291's block, and edits [[§9.9]] and [[R2102000]].

**[[R2321000]] puts a count a card keeps on a Unit into that Unit's view (issue #2321, 2026-10-29)**: the release's screens review (#2204) found that TG #31 Level Up!'s attack counts lived only in its memory, so no screen showed a Unit's progress toward its Radiant; a card's `tallies` hook, a pure read like `preview`, now rides on each Unit it names as `tallies`, public from a face-up card and its controller's alone from a face-down one. It takes R2321000, the first number of issue #2321's block. It edits [[§10.8]] and [[§10.9]].

**[[R2321001]] marks In a hurry's tokens while their end-of-turn destroy waits (issue #2321, 2026-10-29)**: the same review found nothing said TG #15's Rush Tokens die at the end of the turn; each delayed destroy now marks its token with [[R437]]'s mark, `endOfTurn`, in both views. It takes R2321001. It edits [[§10.8]].

**[[R2325000]] drops the provisional banner and the reading notes from /stats (issue #2325, 2026-10-29)**: presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). The statistics page shows no `Provisional statistics` banner and no `How to read these statistics` notes, even while `GET /api/stats/cards` answers with its `source` as `provisional`: the response still sends `source`, `sourceLabel` and `gate`, and the client reads none of them. The patch bar's picker and its previous and next buttons are the way back to the previous patch. It takes R2325000, the first number of issue #2325's block, and amends [[R654]], [[R661]] and [[R1973002]].

**[[R2370000]] adds a Tokens toggle to /stats (issue #2370, 2026-10-29)**: presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). The Cards tab's Show tokens toggle, off by default and carried in the address as `tokens=1`, shows the tokens among `GET /api/stats/cards`' `created` in a section of their own, each with its games, its win rate (the seats that made it, used or not) and its Used and Not used figures, best first. The game record, the totals and the response are unchanged: [[R2089000]] already counts every Created card, tokens among them. It takes R2370000, the first number of issue #2370's block, and amends [[R2089000]].

**[[R2127000]] Back closes the topmost sheet or dialog before it leaves the screen (issue #2127, part of #2036, 2026-10-29)**: the browser's Back and Android's back gesture went to the previous screen with a sheet or dialog open, or met the leave guard. Now they close the overlay opened last first, one per Back, and leave the address and the screen as they were: the card, face-down, pile and stack sheets, the card detail, hero inspect and the pack scene, the command menu, the settings panel, the friends panel, a conversation, the challenge dialog, and the Concede and "Leave this game?" questions, where Back means "Keep playing". A prompt, a play being built and the mulligan never close on Back. Where the browser has `CloseWatcher` the top overlay holds one; elsewhere the client keeps one history entry of its own, which never piles up, never reopens what it closed on Forward and never reaches the leave guard ([[R1754000]]) or a view transition ([[R2117000]]). One entry per overlay was rejected. It takes R2127000, the first number of issue #2127's block, and edits [[§10.10]].

**[[R2205000]] to [[R2205003]] are the tutorial remade from Training Grounds (issue #2205, part 12 of #2193, 2026-10-29)**: the owner asked for a tutorial dealt from the Training Grounds set that cannot be lost and lets a player discover the mechanics themselves, with the set's Legendary and Mythic cards only in the last lesson, and chose five lessons. Each lesson's coach is built around moments. A moment poses a question and is done once the human's view shows the mechanic found; the coach names the rule in a discovery bubble, at most two between the player's moves, and its hints escalate only while the player stalls or asks. A moment waiting for its board lets a later one show first, and a moment never holds the AI ([[R2205000]]). Every lesson is proved won under every way to play it, a reckless player who attacks into everything and guards nothing included ([[R2205001]]). The mechanics found fill a Field Guide kept on the device, where a lesson won counts its mechanics met ([[R2205002]]). The five lessons keep the Core tutorial's four ids and add `finale`; both decks are Training Grounds cards, lessons 1 to 4 hold no Legendary or Mythic card and set the human's pool hold, and lesson 5's human deck holds the set's four ([[R2205003]]). They amend [[R291]], [[R292]], [[R293]] and [[R314]], rewrite [[§9.10]], and take R2205000 to R2205003 from issue #2205's block.

**[[R2206000]] A summon may seed the fresh card's memory, and Trigor's chain is marked that way (issue #2206, part of #2193, 2026-10-29)**: the M13 audit (REVIEW B-1, A-1) found TG #25 Trigor's terminator read a stat override as the mark of a chain body, so a played Trigor that TG #34 Dino-size set to 10/10 and that died the same turn summoned nothing, where §8.11 says a 13/13. §6.3's Summon of a fresh card now carries an optional `memory`, and Trigor's Death marks the Trigor it summons with the turn. It takes R2206000, the first number of issue #2206's block, and edits [[R2197004]] and §8.11 #25.

**[[R2206001]] Lesson 1's AI deck holds units with no keyword whose only text, if any, is a Cry (issue #2206, part of #2193, 2026-10-29)**: the M13 audit (REVIEW B-5) found lesson 1's AI deck holding Aura and Death units and lesson 2's an Aura, against [[§9.10]]. Training Grounds has three units with no text, so lesson 1 holds those and nine Cry units, and every other ability waits for the lesson that teaches it; the test now reads each card's type, keywords and text against the lesson that teaches it. It takes R2206001 and edits [[§9.10]] and [[R2205003]].

**[[R2226000]] keeps a Trap's text fused into a card that is no trap quiet in a unit zone, whatever fused it (issue #2226, 2026-10-28)**: the daily fuzz waves found 饕餮 holding an eaten Wormhole that countered and recast its own controller's Spell for ever, since a Trap's trigger queued as a unit's was never asked its condition ([[R99]]; [[R2271000]] now asks it), and the same trigger reached a Unit through an Equip ([[R1086]]) and a Dormant Starship through a Spacerace Stimulus piece ([[R1852000]]). [[R1020]] said so for 饕餮 alone; this ruling holds it for every Fuse, leaves the card's other texts working and lets a card that is still a trap where it stands ([[R383]]) fire the text as a trap. It takes R2226000, the first number of issue #2226's block.

**[[R2253000]] is the daily quests for Stinkyscape, Radiant upgrades and position switches (issue #2253, 2026-10-28)**: Stinkyscape shipped with no set quest and nothing caught it, and no quest asked for the two moves whose counts every game record already keeps ([[R1256000]]), and Training Grounds ([[R2204000]]) shipped meanwhile with none either, so the pool gains `stinkyscape-8`, `training-grounds-8`, `radiant-upgrade-2` and `switch-5`, the goals `radiantUpgrades` and `positionSwitches` count as the record does, and a unit test holds every set that ships to a set quest. It takes R2253000, the first of issue #2253's block, and edits [[§14.9]].

**[[R2268000]] shows on the viewer's own hand card how many Tributes its play asks now (issue #2268, 2026-10-29)**: the hand hints (issue #2267) needed a number the client cannot work out, since a Float, a Nerf or a Buff moves a printed Tribute X. The view now carries `CardView.tribute`, the number the play is held to, on the viewer's own hand cards only and never at 0, and the client draws it as a small Tribute pip. Working it out in the client was rejected. It takes R2268000, the first number of issue #2268's block, and edits [[§10.8]].

**[[R2280000]] and [[R2280001]] make a backrow card a Unit only with Animated and stats (issue #2280, 2026-10-28)**: the owner reworked Animated so that a Field Spell, Trap or Field Trap becomes a Unit only when it has both the keyword and stats, an attack and health line its definition gives it (printed, fused, a `statsOverride` or X stats), which buffs, auras and tuning never make ([[R2280000]]). Every moment of [[R383]] checks both, the `animate` verb moves an Animated card only and [[R386]]'s stats change reaches a Unit or a card with stats. [[R657]]'s 0/1 for an Animated card with no stats is retired with its two constants. A face-up backrow card that first comes to have both, through a Fuse or a granted Animated, animates at that state check ([[R2280001]]). S #16 Rod of Ages and C+ #74 Twice Forward One Step Backwards lose their printed stats: Rod stays in its backrow zone, lends the Equip play there ([[R1869000]]) and animates once the Unit played onto it fuses in, and Twice Forward animates only once a Unit's copy gives it stats. The change moved the fuzz wave onto a loop already there: a would-die flicker that no firing spends answered every pass of a check, which [[R1884001]] now holds to once a check, as [[R460]] has every replacement.

**[[R2368000]] gives Player 1 three opening cards again (issue #2368, 2026-10-29)**: since issue #1156 both seats drew four ([[R1156000]]), and the owner asked for Player 1's fourth card to go: `OPENING_DRAW` is `[3, 4]` again, Hearthstone's split, still a table indexed by seat. Player 2 keeps The Coin ([[R244]]) and Player 1 the first turn and its turn-1 draw; a handicap's extra cards still come on top ([[R182]]), and Quickdraw cards still replace draws out of the total ([[R640]]). Two 25-card decks that do nothing now fatigue out at player-turn 58, Player 1 winning, where they ended at 57, so the cap of 60 stays a backstop ([[R389]]). A game made while both seats drew four no longer reaches its recorded hash and is refused as `rules_changed` ([[R767000]]), as #1156's own change retired the games before it. It takes R2368000, the first number of issue #2368's block, supersedes [[R1156000]], edits [[§2.1]], [[§2.5]] and [[§13.2]], and amends [[R182]], [[R389]] and [[R640]].

**[[R2378000]] runs one spend at a time per account (issue #2378, 2026-10-29)**: a player spam-clicking the pack panel saw the server fail, since every spend took a database connection and then waited on its account's economy lock ([[R1301003]]) and every press was a new key ([[R2124000]]), so a burst held the pool's connections on one lock until every other checkout timed out. Each of the six economy spends now claims its account's turn before it reads the store and holds it until it answers: `ECONOMY_SPENDS_RUNNING_MAX` run at once, the next ones wait their turn in the server holding no connection and then run, and one sent while `ECONOMY_SPENDS_WAITING_MAX` already wait is refused at once with 429, stating `SPEND_BUSY_RETRY_AFTER_MS` and spending nothing. The owner asked for a wait over an error, so a second tab or a racing retry waits instead of being refused, and the client, keeping a 429's key, holds Buy, Open, Open all, the sealed pack, Reveal all and Next pack while one of its spends is out, Buy and Open turning a loading wheel. Seeding each pack at purchase was rejected: the openings already commit one at a time from fresh seeds, so it would change no result for a migration and a backfill. It takes R2378000, the first of issue #2378's block, edits [[§14.11]] and amends [[R1301003]] and [[R2124000]]. Issue #2610 (2026-10-31) gave the same turn to the other routes that took a connection and then waited on the lock: a card's picks saved or uploaded, a practice seed asked for or given back, and a quest replaced.

**[[R2379000]] gives a practice game's seed back when it is left without saving (issue #2379, 2026-10-29)**: an account holds at most three unclaimed practice seeds for an hour each, and leaving a game without saving never freed its place, so three quit games made the fourth earn nothing, silently: no Jlore, no XP, no quest progress, and nothing said so. Now `DELETE /api/practice/seeds/:seed` deletes the caller's own unclaimed seed under the account's economy lock, freeing its place at once, and the page sends it, keepalive, on Leave without saving and when a new game drops a saved earning one; another account's seed, a claimed one or one never issued answers 404 and changes nothing, and a claim of a released seed is an unknown seed. A game the server would not seed says from its start, and in its result, that it earns nothing, in the server's words. It takes R2379000, the first of issue #2379's block, and edits [[§14.8]].

**[[R2380000]] has the AI price a Dormant card by the unit it wakes as (issue #2380, 2026-10-29)**: [[R1839000]] left a Dormant card out of the AI's evaluation, so the AI almost never played Stinkyscape's Imprisoned Units. The evaluation now counts each Dormant card on the field at the worth of the same card awake, `slumber_turn_share` of it per turn left on its countdown or `slumber_waiting_share` when it waits for an effect; it is still no Unit for every other rule. It takes R2380000, the first number of issue #2380's block, amends [[R1839000]] and edits [[§6.1]] and [[§9.9]].

**[[R2388000]] shows a ranked match's rank move on its result (issue #2388, 2026-10-29)**: a ranked result showed nothing of the rank it moved, and the bar kept the old rank. `GET /api/matches/:matchId/ranks` now carries each seat's `rankBefore` and `rankAfter` off the rated record ([[R611]]), a ranked series' last game the series' ([[R604]]), visible ranks only ([[R612]]); the client re-reads it when the result arrives, retrying briefly, and shows each seat's move in the result panel and the new rank on the bar. It takes R2388000, the first number of issue #2388's block, and edits [[§9.12]] and [[R612]].

**[[R2424000]] has the AI price a unit's Death by what it leaves behind (issue #2424, 2026-10-30)**: the AI's evaluation read a unit whose Death summons a bigger body or adds cards as a plain body, so it never played Training Grounds #25 Trigor. At each decision's root it now prices every Death it can read, on either field and among its own hand's Units, as the controller's evaluation after the sandbox's destroy less after its remove; a unit counts `AI_EVAL.death` of that price and a kill of it the whole price less. It takes R2424000, the first number of issue #2424's block, and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2425000]] has the shadow-ban sweep count only the turns a card could be played (issue #2425, 2026-10-30)**: beside `affordableTurns` the sweep counts `playableTurns`, the AI's turn starts on which `legal_actions` lists a `Play` of the card from its hand, so a Tribute it cannot pay, a missing target or an unmet play condition counts no miss. `neverPlayed`, its at-risk form and its pass-2 ban read the new count against the same thresholds, while unswept still means never affordable, and rows, `--json` lines and reasons show both counts. It takes R2425000, the first number of issue #2425's block, amends [[R186]] and [[R390]] and edits [[§9.9]].

**[[R2427000]] A card's drawn delta: the games it was drawn in against the games it never was (issue #2427, 2026-10-30)**: a seat plays a card when it can afford it and when it is ahead, so [[R377]]'s played-vs-held delta measures the game as much as the card. Never drawn, the decks that held the card and never had it in hand, is in deck less played less drawn but not played, for games, wins and draws alike, read off the totals and never stored; the drawn delta, the drawn win rate (played and drawn but not played together) minus the never-drawn win rate in percentage points, replaces the played delta everywhere a figure is shown — `stats report`, `--json` and `--window`, `GET /api/stats/cards`, the `/stats` column beside When drawn, the card's stats block, the analysis loader and notebook 01 — under [[R654]]'s floor on each side, while `played_delta()`, the API's `playedDelta` field and balance-watch keep the old figure. It takes R2427000, the first number of issue #2427's block, amends [[R377]] and [[R654]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2433000]] and [[R2433001]] are the tutorial opponent playing only Core cards and a lesson deck's copies (issue #2433, part of #2429, 2026-10-30)**: the owner asked that the tutorial's opponent never play a card that isn't a Core card, even one added to its hand. A handicap's `coreOnly`, which `AI_TUTORIAL` sets, bars the seat from playing, setting or casting a card whose set is not Core, however it reached the hand: `legal_actions` lists no such play and `reduce` refuses one, so the AI, the random policy and the timeout all comply; such a card drawn is not cast on draw, and an effect casts or summons none out of that hand. A card the seat holds on the field by other means is left alone, Training Grounds' snapshots of Core cards are not Core, and the field reaches no other game. A handicap's `deckCopies` lets its seat's deck hold that many copies of a card in place of [[§2.6]] L3's one, and `TUTORIAL_HUMAN` sets it to 4, since the first lesson is mostly vanilla Units and Training Grounds has only three with no text ([[R2433001]]). They amend [[R290]] and [[R1012003]] and edit [[§2.6]] and [[§9.10]]; the remade tutorial (issue #2434) deals its opponent Core decks on it.

**[[R2434000]] [[R2434001]] [[R2434002]] [[R2434003]] The tutorial made simpler, one subject a lesson, against a Core-only opponent, with a concede counted as a loss and the coach out of the middle (issue #2434, part of #2429, 2026-10-30)**: the owner's decisions of 2026-10-30 on the five-lesson Training Grounds tutorial. Each lesson teaches one thing: the basics with mostly vanilla units, then Cries and keywords, then spells, then Field Spells and Traps, then everything, TG #39 Call to Chaos (Training-Grounds Edition) and #40 Goetzmann Game Design the player's alone. The human's decks stay Training Grounds cards and may copy a vanilla unit; the opponent's are Core cards, and with #2433's engine rule it plays nothing else; lessons 1 to 4 hold Legendary and Mythic cards out of both seats' pools. Lessons 2 to 4 take new ids, `keywords`, `casting` and `backrow`, and the old `spells`, `traps` and `advanced` read as the lesson in their place, so nothing won is un-won (R2434000). A concede is a loss that the result names and the device keeps (R2434002), and the coach's desktop bubble docks to a corner, never the middle of the board (R2434003). It takes R2434000 to R2434003, the first numbers of issue #2434's block, supersedes [[R2206001]], and edits [[§9.10]], [[R291]], [[R294]], [[R2201000]] and [[R2205003]].

**[[R2436000]] lets a Buff give a Field Spell Immutable (issue #2436, 2026-10-30)**: the owner asked that every Field Spell may gain Immutable from a Buff. A Field Spell's keyword row, Indestructible alone since issue #1748 ([[R1748001]]), now offers Immutable after it, so a Field Spell that deals damage draws among four keywords. A Field Spell made Immutable takes no later Buff or Nerf, and while it is face-up on the field no Spell, Field Spell or Field Trap targets it ([[R23]], [[R781001]]); Units keep [[R21]]'s pool, which leaves Immutable out. It takes R2436000, the first number of issue #2436's block, edits [[§6.1]] and [[§6.3]] and amends [[R386]] and [[R1748001]].

**[[R2446000]] drops the Cost, Rarity, Keywords and Your stats columns and the set Traps line from /stats (issue #2446, 2026-10-30)**: presentation only (CLAUDE.md rule 7). The Cards tab's table offers no Cost, Rarity or Keywords column and no sort by them, since the filter bar already filters by rarity, cost and keyword, and an old address's `?sort=cost`, `rarity` or `keyword` opens on the default sort; the signed-in Your stats column is gone, since every row showed the player's overall win rate rather than the card's; and the Game figures panel no longer shows how many set Traps fired, which told a player nothing, while `GET /api/stats/games` still sends the counts. It takes R2446000, the first number of issue #2446's block, amends [[R1994000]] and edits [[§9.11]].

**[[R2450000]] a Quest card's face prints only the stage it stands at (issue #2450, 2026-10-30)**: each Quest card printed its quest its own way, and none showed the stage it stood at: The Greatest Heist printed its three stages, one `Quest: Stage n, Name. … Reward: …` line each, cut to its first two lines (one on its Radiant face); Job Application its three stages in another layout under an intro line, cut to two or three; and In Too Deep a summary and none of its quests, so the current stage showed only in the board's badge and the inspect overlay's list ([[R1854000]]). Now a staged Quest's face prints its other lines and the one stage the view's line stands at, its quest's progress, its wait to hear back or `Quest complete`, Stage 1 of n off the field, and In Too Deep's its text and its open quests with their rewards; the hover preview, the sheet and the detail view keep the whole text, every stage, and Job Application's stage lines take The Greatest Heist's layout, a change of wording only. Printing each stage's goal from its catalog line alone was rejected: Job Application's Interview draws its goal in play, which only the view names. It takes R2450000, the first of issue #2450's block, edits [[§6.3]], [[§10.8]] and [[§10.10]] and amends [[R1854000]].

**[[R2455000]] raises the bar for a patch (issue #2455, 2026-10-30)**: a patch restarts every card's statistics ([[R1367000]]), so it waits for a change broad enough to move them. `PATCH_MIN_CARDS` goes from 10 to 50 families. Divergences that name no card are a rule every game meets, and make a patch, only once they hold `RULE_MIN_GAMES` (a quarter) of `classify`'s 200 unforced games, or when the base build refuses the head's decks ([[R1705004]]). Fewer count as no card, and the change is tiered by the families it plays. Shipped versions keep their names and tiers. It takes R2455000, the first number of issue #2455's block, and amends [[R1365000]], [[R1366000]], [[R1367000]], [[R1369000]] and [[R1370000]].

**[[R2476000]] shows what a card remembers in its text (issue #2476, 2026-10-30)**: the owner asked that every card that records or remembers some state show it in its text, as #31 KY's Math Equation shows its damage in braces. A pass over the whole catalog held each card to one measure (its text turns on something recorded before now, and the view shows it nowhere on the card) and gave the 33 cards that meet it an [[R280]] `preview`: a count after the words that name it, or the remembered card's name as its `display`, a dash before anything is remembered. A fused card's ingredients each preview what their own text remembered ([[R102]]). It takes R2476000, the first of issue #2476's block, and edits [[§10.8]] and [[R280]].

**[[R2499000]] counts reprinted cards apart from the cards an engine change reaches (issue #2499, 2026-10-30)**: [[R2455000]]'s single bar of 50 families made Meditative balance changes IV, which reprints 19 families, a micro. A change is now a patch when `PATCH_MIN_REPRINTS` (15) families are reprinted (their catalog entry changed and they play differently), when `PATCH_MIN_CARDS` (50) play differently by any route, or when a rule every game meets changed. It takes R2499000, the first number of issue #2499's block, and amends [[R2455000]].

**[[R2529000]] gives a change to how cards look a micro and a system the owner names a patch (issue #2529, 2026-10-30)**: a change under `apps/web/src/cards/` (`CARD_VISUALS`, tests aside) is a Cards micro of no family, claimed by a fragment's `visual` (`patches --visual`). The owner names a system in `SYSTEM_RELEASES` by its tracker issue, and a fragment claiming it (`patches --system <issue>`) ships as a patch, recorded `system: true` when the system alone made it one. Card statistics read such a patch as a micro over the families it plays. It takes R2529000, the first number of issue #2529's block, and amends [[R1365000]], [[R1366000]], [[R1367000]] and [[R1370000]].

**[[R2386000]] A shipped set with no win-rate row CN Tech can pick makes a refresh due from development games (issue #2386, 2026-10-30)**: the compiled win-rate table CN Tech and NA Tech read was v0.4.0's and held no Stinkyscape or Training Grounds row, so CN Tech always gave Lunar Eclipse and NA Tech always gave Infinite Reserves. [[R1368000]]'s refresh had not fired, because patches shipped faster than 1000 live games. The new rule refreshes the table from development games, provisional, while a shipped set has no row either card can pick and the input covers every shipped set, and a cards test fails while a shipped set has none; 400 development games (two runs of 200) refilled it. It takes R2386000, the first number of issue #2386's block, and amends [[R1368000]], [[§8.8]] row 50 and [[§8.9]] row 2.

**[[R2386001]] A game that holds its pools ranks the win-rate table without Legendary and Mythic cards (issue #2386, 2026-10-30)**: the refilled table leads with Palantir, a Legendary, so a tutorial game that held its pools ([[R2201001]]) could be handed it by CN Tech, which the old table's Rare leader and trailer never did; the cards sweep of [[R2201001]] failed on it. The ranking now skips the cards a held pool leaves out, for CN Tech's leader and NA Tech's trailer alike, and names none when none is left. It takes R2386001, the second number of issue #2386's block, and amends [[R2201001]], [[§8.8]] row 50 and [[§8.9]] row 2.

**[[R2396000]] Three attack timings: "When" before, "Whenever" after even if gone, "After" only if still there (issue #2396, follows #2285, 2026-10-29)**: the owner split the timing [[R2285000]] had made one. "When this attacks" resolves before any damage of the attack; "Whenever this attacks" and "Whenever this destroys a Unit" resolve after the attack or the kill, once its combat's state check has run, whether or not the card is still on the field, as the snapshot it fought with and once, a Reborn body answering nothing more; "After" keeps [[R2285001]]'s survival. #32 Prem Panther now draws when it trades, Classic #13 Boots on the Ground moves to "When" and draws or Recruits before the damage, and Meditative #95.1 CN Golem shuffles its CN-Virus though it dies in the combat that killed ([[R1245]]); Badger, Honey Badger, Windfurious Prime and Nature Titan now print "When", Bot Loser and Ravager "After", and play as before. The engine runs "Whenever this attacks" as an `afterAttack` flagged `afterAttackEvenIfGone`, and a gone killer's kill trigger, flagged `evenIfGone`, from the snapshot the state check keeps for that kill. It amends [[R426]] and [[R212]], edits [[§4.2]] and [[§6.2]], and takes R2396000, the first number of issue #2396's block.

**[[R2422000]] settles a practice claim once, after a restart or a failed pay (issue #2422, 2026-10-31)**: a practice claim burned its seed and then ran its check and pay in a task of its own, so its pay was lost three ways, each leaving the seed burned and a later claim answering `already_claimed`: a shutdown's drain closed the store under a settle whose caller had gone, a crash took the settle, or all three tries at the pay failed ([[R1688000]]).
